## 🆕 `p616` — `2026-11-02` — trends, each with the evidence that would refute it

### 🔴 `T616-1` — New permissive education supply now arrives as a **tree of other people's directories**, so licence risk has moved from the *row* to the *composition*

🟢 **Evidence.** `4 of 4` new clean permissive rows this pass are agent-skill packages, not
applications — the third consecutive pass in which the class `p614` first typed supplies all the new
rows. 🔴 And the pass's two defects are both **composition** defects: `616-A`'s package is `MIT` at the
root and ships `26` excluded directories, two of them `NonCommercial` or unlicensed; `616-C`'s
upstream is a `111★`, `217 MB` corpus with no grant file feeding `7` of those directories.

🔵 **Why it matters commercially.** A single licence read per repository was adequate when supply was
applications. On a skill package the root licence describes a **wrapper**, and the deliverable is the
tree. 🟢 The operative consequence is an inversion of triage order: on this class, read the grant
composition **before** evaluating the pedagogy — the pedagogy is usually good and the tree usually is
not.

🔴 **What would refute this.** A pass where new permissive supply returns to self-contained
applications, or where enumerating a skill package's vendored directories reliably finds them all
permissive. 🟡 Three upstream probes refuted it this pass; the honest statement is that the class has
been measured at `n=1` package, so the *frequency* is unestablished even though the *mechanism* is
proven.

### 🟢 `T616-2` — The platform licence field is most informative exactly where it refuses to answer

🟢 **Evidence.** `614-E` measured `NOASSERTION` as `6 of 6` non-permissive and advised spending no
discovery budget on it. 🔴 This pass's load-bearing specimen is `NOASSERTION`
(`key=other`, `name=Other`) and **partially `MIT`** on bytes — the platform field declined to name a
family *because the instrument is non-stock*, which is precisely correct, and more correct than the
title line.

🔵 **Refined claim, not a reversal.** `NOASSERTION` stays low-yield for finding **clean** rows and
becomes high-yield for finding **risk**. `616-E` records the revision in full. 🔴 `NULL` and
`NOASSERTION` must still never be pooled: this pass found `NULL` means *no file* (`616-C`) and
`NOASSERTION` means *a file that needs reading* (`616-A`) — different defects, different budgets.

🟡 **What would refute this.** A population of `NOASSERTION` rows in which none conceals a partial
permissive grant, measured at `n>10`. This pass has `n=1` on the positive side.

### 🔴 `T616-3` — LATAM's supply gap is a **licence-file** gap, not an activity gap

🟢 **Evidence, first-hand for the first time.** The region's flagship open-source AI programme,
`latam-gpt`, is cited twice in this base's own market intel for consortium scale (`8` countries,
`30+` institutions). Enumerated and byte-read today: **`1` of `8` repositories carries a permissive
grant; `7` of `8` carry no licence file at all; `0` of `8` are education-specific.** Eleven
Spanish/Portuguese discovery legs returned one non-nil hit, itself ungranted.

🔵 **Why this is a better finding than the count it replaces.** "No LATAM permissive supply" could not
distinguish regional silence from this base's blindness. 🟢 The census distinguishes them: the
publishing is happening and **the licence file is not**. That changes the remedy — a regional
engagement is more likely to unblock supply by contributing a `LICENSE` upstream than by waiting for
new repositories.

🔴 **What would refute this.** A LATAM-published, permissive, education-specific repository above this
base's bar — which would close `616-H` outright. 🟡 Also refutable in the other direction: if
`latam-gpt`'s repositories gain licence files, the cause stated here expires and should be re-measured
rather than carried.

### 🟡 `T616-4` — Star rank remains an anti-signal for commercial usability in education, now on a fourth channel

🟢 **Evidence.** [`JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor`](https://github.com/JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor),
`29,548★` — the most-starred standalone tutor in the sector — byte-read again today: **ladder
exhausted, no grant**. `p601`/`605-J` established this; a fourth independent channel now agrees.
🔴 `616-C` extends the pattern down-rank: `111★` and `217 MB` of the best education-specific skill
content measured on this base, also ungranted.

🔵 **The usable form of the rule.** Rank orders *attention*, not *licence hygiene*, and in this sector
the two appear mildly **anti**-correlated: the biggest education repositories are
prompt/content artefacts, which is where a permissive default is least reliable (`614-G`'s point about
copy versus code, unchanged).

🔴 **What would refute this.** A measured correlation across the top `20` education repositories
showing permissive grants concentrated at the top. 🟡 Never attempted on this base; the claim rests on
individually-read rows, and is stated as a tendency rather than a coefficient.

### 🔵 `T616-5` — Demand-side governance is the best-evidenced engagement surface, and it is not a model problem

🟢 **Evidence.** North America: a Gallup study (April `2026`, secondary) reports **over `60 %` of
college students using AI weekly** while **roughly half of institutions discourage or prohibit it**,
against `134` bills in `31` states. EMEA: updated EU ethical guidelines on AI in education plus
`€25.4 M` in Advanced Digital Skills grants, with education classed Annex III high-risk. LATAM: UNESCO
IESALC/UNU-IAS mapping adoption across five institutional axes.

🟢 **The shape is consistent across all three regions: usage has outrun policy.** 🔵 That is a
governance, assessment-redesign and disclosure problem — none of which is solved by a better tutor
model, and all of which is billable.

🔴 **What would refute this, and the standing weakness.** Every figure above is **secondary**:
`598-G`'s hosts (`unesco.org`, `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `oecd.org`) do not resolve from this environment,
so no primary text was read. 🔴 `614-C`'s cost is unchanged — the EU Annex III primary text has never
been read on this base, and the settled compliance date (**`2027-12-02`**, `Reg. (EU) 2026/1744`) is
carried from `p233`/`p241` rather than re-verified here.

## 🆕 `p615` — `2026-11-02` — trends, each with the evidence that would refute it

### 🔴 `T615-1` — The binding constraint on open-source education AI is the **grant instrument**, and the sector's own curators keep getting it wrong at the clause level

🟢 **Evidence.** This base — purpose-built for exactly this check — published a `CC-BY-NC-4.0`
dataset as 🟢 *"fit for purpose"* for commercial delivery last pass, and in the same table
over-restricted two `CC-BY-SA-4.0` rows whose text permits commercial use *"even commercially"*
twice. Both errors came from identifying the **family** (`CC`) and not the **clause** (`NC` vs `SA`).
🔴 **Refuted if** a population read shows family-level classification is sufficient to decide
shippability. 🟢 It is not: `CC-BY-NC` and `CC-BY-SA` share a family and sit on **opposite sides** of
the commercial question. `evidence/cc-instrument-resolution.2026-11-02-p615.tsv`.

### 🔴 `T615-2` — A curated licence column is not evidence, and neither is a curated **address**

🟢 **Evidence.** `p611` measured `1 of 5` permissive claims surviving a byte read on a standard
higher-ed index. This pass extends the failure mode from *figures* to *identifiers*: Wikipedia's xAPI
library table names `adlnet/xAPI-Java`, and `git ls-remote` does not resolve it. Trend blogs quoted
`DeepTutor` at `52,783★` and `48,268★` against a live `54.1k`.
🔴 **Refuted if** a secondary source's licence, star count and repository name all reproduce
first-hand across a sample. 🟢 Across `615`, none of the three has.

### 🟢 `T615-3` — The sector's head is **saturated**; its new supply enters below every sensible star bar

🟢 **Evidence.** First enumeration independent of WebSearch: `topics/ai-tutor` (`849` stated) and
`topics/education-ai` (`70` stated), `60` rows read, `22` distinct addresses at or above `20★`,
**`22 of 22` already shelved, `0` new**. Re-sorted by freshness, `17 of 20` fall **below** `20★`,
nine at `0★`.
🔴 **Refuted if** an enumeration channel returns addresses this base does not hold. 🟢 This one
returned none — which is why `45` consecutive leg nils is now read as **saturation of the head**
rather than channel blindness. 🔵 The corollary: growth is in the sub-`20★` tail, so the bar is a
visible choice with a known cost.

### 🟢 `T615-4` — Supply is shifting from applications to **prompt text**, which moves the licence question from code to copy

🟢 **Evidence.** `p614` recorded `4` new `MIT` skill packages at `20`–`45★` and the class's largest
member at `~36.7k★`. This pass's enumeration found the same class dominating both topic surfaces
(`JuneYaooo/lineage-skill` `466`, `ZeKaiNie/universal-examprep-skill` `364`,
`SenmuuuuW/universal-diagnostic-tutor-skill` `256`, `KeWang0622/kaogong-skill` `216`,
`flysheep-ai/education-skills` `121`, `bobuel/bloom-taxonomy-quiz-builder-skill`,
`tanghua-git/skill-instructional-design`).
🔴 **Refuted if** the licence family distribution on skill packages matches that of applications.
🟡 It does not: the class's most-trending member (`GarethManning/education-agent-skills`, `942★`) is
`CC-BY-SA-4.0` — a **share-alike obligation on text a studio pastes into a client's agent**. 🟢 And
`615-C` adds the sub-finding that its grant file is a **deed summary, not the legal code**.

### 🟡 `T615-5` — The permissive full-LMS option exists and the sector's own discovery channels do not surface it

🟢 **Evidence.** This pass's ERP/platform leg stated outright that no education-specific MIT/Apache
platform was found, offering only copyleft incumbents. `OpenOLAT` is `Apache-2.0` on bytes
(`10,982 B`, `master`, byte-read `2026-11-02`) with `QTI 2.1` + `SCORM 1.2` + `LTI 1.3` built in.
🔴 **Refuted if** `OpenOLAT`'s grant reads copyleft on bytes, or if its standards support is
vendor-claimed only. 🟢 The grant reproduces; the standards claim is still **vendor-stated** and is
flagged as such.

### 🟢 `T615-6` — The interoperability tier is permissive at the backbone and **archived at the edges**

🟢 **Evidence.** `adlnet` org census: `8` live rows byte-read (`7` `Apache-2.0`, `1` `MIT`), and
~two-thirds of the `30` listed source repositories archived — including the canonical
`SCORM-to-xAPI-Wrapper` (`99★`, archived **and** ungranted on `7` probed names) and
`Unity-xAPI-Wrapper` (`32★`), the tier's only game-engine binding.
🔴 **Refuted if** maintained permissive alternatives exist for the SCORM bridge and the engine
binding. 🟡 This pass did not find them and **did not run a dedicated leg for them** — stated as an
open question, not a conclusion.

### 🟢 `T615-7` — Adoption leads governance in every region measured, and the gap is the procurable artefact

🟢 **Evidence.** LATAM: `87 %` of HEIs use AI, `26 %` have any formal framework (UNESCO IESALC,
`200` HEIs / `19` countries) — a `61`-point gap; Brazil `53 %` vs `22 %` at school level (Cetic.br).
North America: `~60 %` of US teachers use AI (Gallup, `n ≈ 2,200`) while most public schools lack a
formal student policy; `134` bills in `31` states; `4` states now mandate district policy.
APAC: Korea's AI Basic Act in force, Vietnam classifying education as high-risk, TEQSA requiring
plans from **all** Australian providers. EMEA: EU AI Act enforcement live since `2026-08-02`.
🔴 **Refuted if** any region shows governance leading adoption. 🟢 None measured does.

### 🔴 `T615-8` — Measurement channels are not stable between passes, so a pass that does not state its channel cannot be audited

🟢 **Evidence.** `p539` recorded `api.github.com` as `HTTP 403` to this egress; `p614` compared
figures *"with the API"*; this pass measures `403` again on both `api.github.com` and `gh api`, and
`403` on `github.com` HTML via curl. `raw.githubusercontent.com` and `git ls-remote --symref` answer.
🟢 Consequence, applied rather than noted: **no exact star cell appears anywhere in `p615`** — every
figure is a rounded display value, and every grant is byte-exact.
🔴 **Refuted if** star cells can be reproduced first-hand on this egress. 🟢 They cannot, and the
honest move is to publish the rounded figure labelled rounded rather than inherit a precise-looking
number from a prior pass's different channel. `evidence/channel-availability.2026-11-02-p615.tsv`.

## 🆕 `p614` — `2026-11-02` — trends, each with the evidence that would refute it

### 🟢 `T614-1` — education's new permissive supply is **prompt text riding somebody else's agent**, not applications

🟢 **Evidence.** `4` new rows this pass are skill packages for an existing coding agent, not programs:
`YujxZJCN/teaching-skills` (`45★`, a professor's teaching lifecycle), `Kaiji-Z/dsh-plugin-lookatstudy`
(`23★`, repo → guided course with BKT mastery and SM-2 reviews), `smashburger-dev/socratic-workshop`
(`23★`, learn-by-explaining), `tong666-bit/liu-xiaoyan-skill` (`20★`, one instructor's method) — every
one `MIT` byte-read `2026-11-02`. 🔵 And the class's largest member was **already on this shelf
unrecognised**: `virgiliojr94/book-to-skill` at `36,751★`. 🟢 `613-E`'s `CC-BY-SA` specimen,
`GarethManning/education-agent-skills` (`942★`), is the same class — so the class spans `20★` to
`36,751★` and this base has `6` members of it.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A pass that finds the next tranche of permissive education supply arriving
as deployable applications again, with the skill-package rows going quiet (no pushes, no star growth)
within two or three passes. 🔵 The cheap check: re-read the `6` addresses' push dates next pass.

🟡 **Why it matters commercially.** The deliverable stops being a deployment and becomes copy. 🟢 That
collapses delivery cost and crosses borders with no data-residency argument. 🔴 It also moves the
licence question from code to text, where a permissive default is least reliable — `1 of 6` known
members is share-alike.

### 🟢 `T614-2` — the sector's buying question in `2026` is **"may we deploy it, and can we prove it"**

🟢 **Evidence.** `5` of this pass's `8` mandated legs returned regulatory substance and `0` returned a
market figure this base did not already hold. Specifically: `134` US state bills across `31` states
with `MD`/`ID`/`OK`/`VA` mandating district-level policy; Korea's AI Basic Act in force `2026-01-22`;
Vietnam's `134/2025/QH15` from `2026-03-01` **naming education high-risk**; Australia's TEQSA
requiring an institutional gen-AI action plan from every provider; UNESCO IESALC measuring `87 %`
adoption against `26 %` with any framework. 🔵 Independent corroboration from the leg prose itself:
OECD's `2026` Digital Education Outlook recommends moving *beyond general-purpose AI tools toward
purpose-built educational AI*, and `1EdTech`/HolonIQ both frame `2026` as governance and proven
instructional benefit rather than capability.

🔴 **What would refute it.** Procurement evidence that institutions are still buying on capability —
for instance a pass where the regional legs return vendor-selection news and no new statutory dates.

🟢 **The inventory this base holds for it**, all permissive and all already shelved: `612-E`'s
rubric/autograding tier as conformity evidence, `613-B`'s `Apache-2.0` xAPI/cmi5 **conformance test
suites** (US DoD ADL), `613-C`'s LRS + statement simulator so a stack is demonstrable before a single
real learner exists. 🔵 That is an unusually good fit: the sector is asking for proof, and the
permissive supply is disproportionately *proof infrastructure*.

### 🟢 `T614-3` — a platform licence field that **declines** is not a licence fact in either direction, and the two ways it declines mean opposite things

🟢 **Evidence.** `10` rows byte-read where the field declined: `NULL` (`4 of 4`) had **no grant file at
all** across an `18`-path ladder; `NOASSERTION` (`6 of 6`) had a **real grant file and not one was
permissive** — `2` Creative Commons, `1` Elastic License `2.0`, `1` bespoke non-commercial, `2`
cross-repo pointers resolving to `AGPL-3.0`. 🔵 Against this, where the field makes a *positive*
assertion it reproduced on bytes `18 of 18` this pass and `110 of 110` pooled with `612`/`613`.

🟢 **Operative split, which `613-E` could not make from `n = 1`:** for *discovery*, `NOASSERTION` has
a `0 %` permissive yield — do not spend search budget on it. For *shipping*, byte-read it always.
🔴 And never pool `NULL` with `NOASSERTION` again.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A `NOASSERTION` row that byte-reads to `MIT` or `Apache-2.0` — which is
entirely possible and simply did not occur in `6` cases. 🔵 The honest statement of strength is
`0 of 6`, not "never".

### 🟢 `T614-4` — licence-metadata defects are **owner-shaped**, and that is now the base's second independent confirmation

🟢 **Evidence.** `604-A` carried *"the API is wrong about `ECL-2.0` in `10 of 11` repos"* as a property
of the licence; measured here, `opencast/annotation-tool` is a **fifth publisher outside the four
Apereo orgs that sweep covered, and the field is correct there**. 🔵 Second, independent instance in
the same pass: the `121 B` pointer `LICENSE.md` of `614-F` is **byte-identical across two repositories
of one owner** — an owner-level convention, not two coincidences. 🟢 Both point the same way as
`613-A`, which found `12` dead addresses recovering under one owner and adopted the rule *group by
owner before writing anything off*. 🔴 `n = 1` outside Apereo, so this **bounds** `604-A`'s number
rather than replacing it.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A sixth `ECL-2.0` publisher, outside Apereo, where the field is wrong.

### 🟢 `T614-5` — Creative Commons is the **right** family for the sector's scarcest asset and the wrong one for its newest

🟢 **Evidence.** `xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy` (`6,143★`, `CC`) is a curriculum-aligned knowledge
graph: `1,644` knowledge points, `2,403` prerequisite edges, `6,723` problems. 🔵 A prerequisite graph
is the scarcest input an adaptive-learning engagement has and is *data*, where `CC` is the conventional
and correct family. 🔴 The same family on `613-E`'s agent-skill repository is a share-alike obligation
on prompt text a studio pastes into a client's agent. 🟢 **So the grade belongs to the asset, not the
licence** — and a filter that drops every `CC` row loses the sector's best dataset.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A client engagement blocked by the attribution or share-alike terms of a
`CC` **dataset** — which would mean the data/copy distinction is thinner than this trend claims.
🔴 Stated limit: this pass's grader returns `CC-?` without a version on all three `CC` rows; the
version must be read before any delivery.

### 🔴 `T614-6` — a "forty-fourth consecutive nil" can be true and still be measuring the wrong thing

🟢 **Evidence.** All `8` mandated legs returned no new permissive supply, the forty-fourth such
result. 🔴 In the same hour, the platform's own search index yielded `32` addresses absent from every
sell shelf, `15` permissive by field, **`13` promoted after a byte read** — a `22.0 %` promotion rate
against the legs' `0 %`. 🔵 And `57 of 59` swept addresses were already cited somewhere in this base,
so the yield is not discovery of unseen names but **adjudication of a backlog**, which is `612-A`'s
finding restated from a new direction.

🟢 **Ask, registered.** Report **channel yield** beside the leg nil every pass. A statistic that has
read `nil` forty-four times while the base's actual supply arrived through an unmeasured channel is
not a finding about the sector; it is a finding about the legs.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A pass where the web legs out-yield the platform index.

## 🆕 `p613` — `2026-11-02` — trends, each with the evidence that would refute it

### 🟢 `T613-1` — the permissive supply in education is concentrating in **seams and standards**, not in tutors

🟢 **Evidence.** Two consecutive passes found whole permissive *tiers* that this base had never shelved,
and neither is an application: `612-D` LTI `1.3` (`5` stacks, `25`–`203★`), `613-B` xAPI/cmi5
conformance (`9` repos, `12`–`956★`, `Apache-2.0`, US DoD), `613-C` LRS + simulation (`5` repos),
`613-D` FSRS scheduling (`8` stacks, `107`–`4,122★`). 🔵 Meanwhile `611` passes of searching for tutors
produced rows that top out around `450★` and are mostly single-author.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A permissive, multi-contributor, `>1,000★` education *application* —
tutor, LMS or courseware — appearing and staying maintained. 🔵 `fsrs4anki` at `4,122★` is the closest
thing on these shelves and it is a **scheduler**, which is the point.

### 🔴 `T613-2` — star rank in education AI does not track licence usability

🟢 **Evidence.** The highest-starred education-agent repository either search leg returned this pass is
`GarethManning/education-agent-skills` at `942★`, and it is 🔴 `CC-BY-SA-4.0` on bytes — share-alike, on
prompt text (`613-E`). 🔵 The two `>76,000★` rows the legs returned are AI curricula, not education
software. 🟢 By contrast the pass's most commercially useful row, `yetanalytics/lrsql`, has `144★`.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A measurement over a larger population showing permissive share rising with
stars in this sector. 🟡 **Stated limit:** `T613-2` currently rests on `~5` addresses and is a
*hypothesis with one hard case*, not a rate. The census that would settle it is cheap and is left
registered rather than claimed.

### 🟢 `T613-3` — governments are now a primary source of permissive education code, in four jurisdictions

🟢 **Evidence, one new jurisdiction per pass for three passes.** `611-D` Government of British Columbia
(`bcgov/SIMS`, `Apache-2.0`); `612-F` UK Department for Education (three live national services, `MIT`)
and Oak National Academy (`MIT`); `613-B` US Department of Defense ADL Initiative (`adlnet`, `92`
repositories, `Apache-2.0`); `613-C` France Université Numérique (`openfun/ralph`, `MIT`, pushed
`2026-11-01`).

🔵 **Why it is a trend and not a coincidence.** All four publish under **permissive** grants while both
dominant incumbent platforms are copyleft — states optimise for adoption and reuse, vendors for
control. 🔴 **What would refute it:** a state education body relicensing to `AGPL`, or the next two
passes finding no new jurisdiction.

### 🔴 `T613-4` — a curated index must be classified before it is mined, and the test is one ratio

🟢 **Evidence.** `613-H` censused four education indexes. `GeminiLight/awesome-ai-llm4education`:
`181` arXiv links, `7` GitHub addresses, `1` permissive — a **bibliography**.
`open-spaced-repetition/awesome-fsrs`: `0` arXiv, `54` addresses, `32` permissive — a **supply index**.
🔵 The `arXiv : GitHub` ratio separates the two cleanly on all four.

🔴 **What would refute it.** An index with a high paper ratio that nonetheless yields shippable supply.
🟡 `n = 4`.

### 🟢 `T613-5` — LATAM's binding constraint is **governance**, not content or architecture

🟢 **Evidence.** Digital Education Council × Tec de Monterrey `2026`: `92 %` of students and `79 %` of
faculty already active, faculty positive opinion `57 % → 72 %`. UNESCO IESALC, `200` institutions in
`19` countries: adoption–governance gap most pronounced in the region; *no formal institutional AI
policies*. 🔵 Against `0 of 24` promoted rows and `0 of 90` index addresses placing to LATAM.

🔵 **This revises `612-5`, which read the gap as Spanish/Portuguese content.** 🟢 At `92 %` adoption the
demand-side work is done; the missing artefact is institutional policy, and it is missing the *same way*
in `200` institutions — which makes it a template, not a bespoke build.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A LATAM-published permissive education repository above `100★`, or survey
evidence that institutional policies are in fact widespread. 🔵 `611-F`'s Angolan `kifula` (`MIT`, `0★`)
is the only Lusophone supply signal this base has ever had.

### 🔴 `T613-6` — five consecutive passes have found their central defect in the **instrument**

🟢 **Evidence.** `610-E`, `611-B` (every `GPL-2.0` file mis-graded as `LGPL`), `611-I` (an upstream step
that never fired), `612-C` (a case-sensitive ladder grading a `67,644★` MIT repo ungranted), `613-F` (a
batched existence probe that fails silently in two distinct ways). 🟢 All five were caught **before
publication**.

🔵 **The reading that matters for delivery.** On this corpus the dominant error source is no longer the
data; it is the tooling built to read it. 🟢 So the transferable deliverable for a client is **the
verified ladder and its controls**, not the row list — the rows go stale in weeks, and `612-A` measured
`11.9 %` of a carried backlog unresolvable within one quarter. 🔴 **What would refute it:** two
consecutive passes whose central correction is a corpus fact.


## 🆕 `p612` — `2026-11-02` — trends, each with the evidence that would refute it

### 🟢 `T612-1` — licence metadata is not uniformly unreliable; **curated** licence metadata is

🟢 Measured both layers on comparable corpora, all grants byte-read at the real default branch: a curated
third-party index managed **`1 of 5`** permissive claims (`p611`); the platform's own `license.spdx_id`
managed **`68 of 68`** (`100 %`), with `0` contradictions and `0` branch disagreements.
🟢 **Consequence:** triage on the platform field, byte-read at the point of shipping, never trust a
curated index. 🔴 **What would refute it:** a single contradicted row in a larger platform-field sample —
`68` is not many, and this measures reproduction of *permissive* claims only, not the API's recall. The
`157` `NULL` and `68` `NOASSERTION` rows in the same sweep are a different failure and are unmeasured.
Evidence `evidence/api-vs-bytes-grants.2026-11-02-p612.tsv`.

### 🟢 `T612-2` — in education the **integration seam** is the scarce asset, not the model

🔵 Both incumbents are copyleft (Moodle `GPL-3.0`, Open edX `AGPL-3.0`), so a client's proprietary code
cannot live inside them; it has to attach via **LTI**. 🟢 `612-D` found a complete permissive LTI tier —
one implementation per major stack — sitting unshelved in the backlog at `25`–`203★`, two orders of
magnitude below the tutoring demos that trend. 🔴 **What would refute it:** an engagement that shipped
education software at institutional scale without an LTI or equivalent SIS/LMS seam. 🔵 This is `601`'s
star-rank-as-anti-signal finding in its strongest form yet.

### 🟢 `T612-3` — the AI Act's deferral created a `16`-month window, and the deliverable in it is the conformity file

🟢 `(EU) 2026/1744` moved Annex III education high-risk from `2026-08-02` to **`2027-12-02`**; the
obligations themselves are unchanged, and Article `50` transparency is 🔴 **already live** (with
watermarking due `2026-12-02`). 🟢 **Consequence:** the market will read the deferral as permission to
wait and will need risk management, data governance, documentation and human oversight retrofitted onto
systems not built to emit them. 🟢 `612-E`'s rubric tier plus `fairlearn` makes the evidence a
by-product. 🔴 **What would refute it:** a further deferral, or guidance narrowing education's Annex III
scope. 🟡 Secondary sources only — the OJ text was not read from this session.

### 🟢 `T612-4` — the state is the reference implementation in this sector

🟢 Two consecutive passes, two jurisdictions, both permissive: `bcgov/SIMS` (`611-D`, `Apache-2.0`,
provincial system of record with a business-rules engine) and now **three live UK DfE national services
under `MIT`** plus Oak National Academy's `MIT` lesson-planning assistant (`612-F`). 🔵 Government
education code is permissive because procurement rules push it there, and it is *operational* code — a
national admissions pipeline, not a demo. 🟢 **Consequence:** in EMEA and North America the cheapest
credibility in a pitch is a working public-sector reference. 🔴 **What would refute it:** finding these
repositories to be published-but-abandoned mirrors — checked: three of the four were pushed
`2026-11-02`.

### 🟢 `T612-5` — APAC's winning shape is cited retrieval over an authoritative local document set

🟢 `6 of 9` region-placed rows this pass are APAC, and three of them are the **same** architecture:
`campusask` (IIIT Delhi rules), `uajy-academic-rag-chatbot` (Universitas Atma Jaya Yogyakarta handbook)
and `taiwan-exam` (學測 papers) — retrieval restricted to official documents, answers carrying page-level
citations. 🔵 The generic tutor does not win where the exam is national; the one that knows the local
curriculum does. 🟢 Korea's walked-back digital-textbook rollout says the same thing from the failure
side: consent and data posture are shipping dependencies, and offline/on-device is the mitigation.
🔴 **What would refute it:** a generic multilingual tutor achieving national adoption in an APAC market
without curriculum-specific grounding.

### 🔴 `T612-6` — a carried backlog decays, and it decays fastest where the value is

🔴 `101 of 848` education-shaped backlog addresses (`11.9 %`) no longer resolve at all since `p610`
enumerated them — deleted, renamed or made private. 🟢 Only `8.0 %` of the enumerated `848` were
shippable-shaped to begin with, so **the usable fraction and the decay rate are the same order of
magnitude.** 🔴 **Consequence:** carrying a backlog is not free storage; it is a wasting asset, and the
rows that vanish are disproportionately the small, new ones — exactly the band where education's usable
code lives. 🟢 Sweep on enumeration. 🔴 **What would refute it:** a re-sweep showing most of the `101`
were renames recoverable by redirect — not attempted here, and recorded as an open gap.

### 🔴 `T612-7` — the instrument fails more often than the corpus, and it is now four passes running

🔴 `610-E` (missing `COPYING.txt` rung), `611-B` (window substring instead of title line), `611-I`
(upstream detection wrong twice), and this pass's `612-C`: **a case-sensitive ladder that graded a
`67,644★` `MIT` repository "ungranted"** and would have produced a false and highly quotable headline.
🟢 Each was caught by the same discipline — **re-run the whole corpus after the fix and report what
moved**; here exactly one row moved. 🔵 `612`'s own experience adds a mechanism: the defect was found
because committed-instrument execution was denied and the gate had to be **re-authored line by line**.
🔴 **What would refute it:** a pass whose corpus error rate exceeds its instrument error rate. Not yet
observed on this base.

## 🆕 `p611` — `2026-11-02` — trends, each with the evidence that would refute it

### 🔴 `T611-1` — a curated licence column is not evidence, and the error rate is now measured

🔵 **Claim.** Third-party indexes — the awesome-lists and comparison tables every engagement reads
first — misstate licences at a rate high enough to change build decisions, **in both directions**.

🟢 **Evidence.** The sector's standard higher-education platform index, censused row by row against
grant bytes at the real default branch: `20` rows, 🟢 `10` reproduce, 🔴 `4` contradicted, 🟡 `5`
unverifiable (no grant payload at all), `1` non-OSI and correctly labelled. 🔴 **Of the `5` rows
claiming a *permissive* grant, `1` survives** — one is `AGPL-3.0` at `84★`; three have no `LICENSE` at
any ladder rung. 🟢 And one row published as `GPL-3.0` is actually `MIT`.
`evidence/curated-licence-census.2026-11-02-p611.tsv`.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A second index censused the same way reproducing `> 90 %`. 🔵 Carried as
`611-H-open`: `n = 1` index. The *rate* is measured; its **generality across indexes is not**.

### 🟢 `T611-2` — the regulatory deadline is now the buying trigger in every region, and `p611` adds two jurisdictions

🔵 **Claim.** `T610-1` said three regions carry a dated statutory obligation. 🟢 Refined: it is now
**four**, and the newest two name education *explicitly* rather than by implication.

| region | the dated thing | what it forces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| North America | `4` states mandate **district** AI policy; DC bars AI grading & discipline `2026-27` | artefact: a policy enforced in code |
| EMEA | Reg. (EU) 2026/1744 — high-risk `2027-12-02`; Art. 4 literacy duty **live now** | ~13-month build window, live literacy duty |
| 🆕 APAC | 🆕 **Vietnam `33/2026/QD-TTg`** — education `1` of `6` high-risk sectors, **automated assessment** named | assessment features become regulated features |
| 🆕 APAC | 🆕 **Australia TEQSA** — *every* HE provider files a gen-AI risk action plan | a deliverable, per institution, now |
| LATAM | `716` instruments across `23` countries, `> 80 %` since 2023 | no single compliance target |

🔴 **What would refute it.** A region where procurement is still led by capability pilots with no
dated obligation in sight. 🔵 On this pass's evidence there is none.

### 🔴 `T611-3` — in education, high search novelty and low usable yield are the same measurement

🔵 **Claim.** The discovery channel is **diluted, not dry**. `976` addresses over `19` axes returned
🟢 `91.6 %` new to a `3,758`-address base and 🔴 only `4.7 %` new-permissive-and-`≥ 10★`, with 🔴 `14 of
19` axes yielding **zero** shippable-shaped rows.

🔵 **Why this matters more than another nil.** It reconciles `p600`'s `80.3 %` novelty against `p599`'s
`0 %` in a single table: 🟢 **novelty and value are independent axes**, and a generic education axis
surfaces the long tail of single-author coursework. 🔴 The modal "new" address is a `0★` CRUD project.

🔴 **What would refute it.** An axis returning high novelty **and** high permissive yield at a usable
star floor. 🟢 Five axes paid at all this pass, and the one that generalises is 🔵 **a local-language
axis phrased as a school subject** (`pendidikan AI indonesia`) — `p600`'s "jobs, not technologies"
restated for language.

### 🔴 `T611-4` — the governance deficit, not the capability gap, is the sellable unit — and LATAM quantifies it

🔵 **Claim.** Demand for education AI is now **governed demand**: institutions are already using AI and
are unable to evidence how.

🟢 **Evidence, LATAM, with a `19`-country denominator.** `87 %` of ~`200` HEIs use AI in at least one
area; 🔴 `26 %` have a formal strategy; 🔴 `< 10 %` have guidelines *and* the capacity to apply them;
🔴 `55 %` have **no AI budget**, and 🔴 public universities **`4 %`**. Brazil: `53 %` of administrators
use AI, 🔴 `22 %` of schools have guidelines (Cetic.br). Uruguay: `75 %` of public-school teachers use
the tools (Ceibal).

🔵 **The same shape outside LATAM:** 🔴 `55 %` of US high-school principals have not restricted AI on
the network while `60 %` of teachers use it (Gallup); most US public schools have no formal student
policy. 🟢 **So the deficit is global and LATAM is merely the best-measured instance** — which is the
opposite of the usual direction of this base's regional evidence.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A survey showing strategy adoption converging on usage. 🟡 None found.

### 🟢 `T611-5` — the permissive supply in education is the public sector and the periphery, never the core

🔵 **Claim.** Restated with this pass's two additions and now hard to dislodge: **every education
platform of record is copyleft**, and permissive grants cluster in (a) government-published systems
and (b) tools *around* the LMS.

🟢 **Evidence.** 🔴 Copyleft core: `moodle/moodle` `GPL-3.0`, `OpenEMIS/core` `GPL-2.0` (corrected this
pass), `openeducat_erp` `LGPL-3.0`, `nomadicjosh/eduTrac-SIS` `GPL-3.0`, `hrshadhin/osums` `AGPL-3.0`.
🟢 Permissive: `bcgov/SIMS` **`Apache-2.0`** — a *ministry's* system of record with a business-rules
engine; `moodleapp` / `Azure/Moodle` (mobile and deployment, `p610`); `QkeleQ10/Study-Tools` (`MIT`,
augments the Dutch `Magister` SIS from outside).

🔵 **The actionable form:** 🟢 **the permissive layer is where the integration sits, and the public
sector is where the permissive *core* comes from.** An engagement that needs to own its core should
start from a government publication, not from the market leader.

🔴 **What would refute it.** A major LMS or SIS of record relicensing permissively. 🟡 `610` found the
Moodle *periphery* moving that way, which is weak evidence in the claim's favour, not against it.

### 🔴 `T611-6` — a superseded compliance date is circulating, and it inverts the EMEA sales motion

🔵 **Claim.** Secondary EMEA guidance still publishes *"high-risk education obligations from August
2026."* 🔴 That date was superseded by Reg. (EU) 2026/1744: stand-alone Annex III moved to
🟢 `2027-12-02`, embedded-in-product to 🟢 `2028-08-02`.

🔵 **Why it is a trend and not a footnote.** 🔴 An engagement working from August 2026 sells **urgency
that has passed**; one working from the real timetable sells a **~13-month build window** — while the
emotion-recognition **prohibition** and the Art. 4 **literacy duty** are enforceable *today*. 🟢 The
correct pitch is therefore "ban-compliance and literacy now, high-risk conformity by late 2027," and
anyone pitching an August-2026 cliff is reading a stale guide.

🔴 **What would refute it.** The Omnibus text not carrying those dates. 🔴 `598-G` prevents a primary
read from this host; 🟡 two independent secondary channels agree, and `p610` confirmed `2027-12-02`
from a second channel already.

## 🆕 `p610` — `2026-11-02` — trends, each with the evidence that would refute it

### 🟢 `T610-1` — the regulatory deadline has replaced the budget cycle as the buying trigger

🔵 Three regions now carry a **dated statutory obligation** on education AI, and the dates are close
enough to plan against rather than forecast:

| region | instrument | date | status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 EMEA | emotion-recognition ban in education, Art. 5(1)(f) | `2025-02-02` | 🟢 **in force** |
| 🟢 EMEA | AI transparency duties | `2026-08-02` | 🟢 **in force** |
| 🟢 APAC (India) | IT Rules synthetic-content labelling | `2026-02-20` | 🟢 **in force** |
| 🟢 NA (Ohio) | mandatory district AI policy | `2026-07-01` | 🟢 **in force** |
| 🟡 EMEA | education **high-risk** duties, Reg. (EU) 2026/1744 | `2027-12-02` | 🟡 deferred, dated |

🔴 **What would refute it:** a region where adoption spend is growing while no dated instrument
exists. **LATAM is that case** — `>50 %` teacher adoption against `<10 %` institutional guidelines —
so the trend is *region-conditional*, not universal, and that is how it is recorded.

### 🟢 `T610-2` — provenance labelling is still the single most portable build target

🔵 Reaffirmed from `T607-1` with a third jurisdiction's date verified: **India `2026-02-20`**, **China
since `2025-09-01`**, **EU since `2026-08-02`**. 🟢 Three unrelated legal traditions — a common-law
federal republic, a party-state and a supranational regulator — converge on **labelling
machine-generated content**. 🔴 They converge on nothing else: China prescribes algorithms, the EU
grades by risk tier, India amends intermediary rules.

🟢 **Consequence for a build:** a labelling/provenance layer is the one component that ships unchanged
into all three markets. Everything above it needs regional rework.

### 🟢 `T610-3` — governance inverts adoption everywhere, and only LATAM publishes both sides

🟢 Corroborated in all four regions this pass, with different numbers and the same sign:

| region | adoption | governance | ratio |
| --- | --- | --- | --: |
| 🟢 LATAM | `>50 %` of teachers (Chile, Brazil) | `<10 %` of institutions have guidelines | 🔴 **`>5×`** |
| 🟢 NA | AI use in schools "growing" across trackers | `33`–`35` of `51` jurisdictions publish guidance | 🟡 ~`1.5×` |
| 🟢 APAC | `2` states with compulsory national curricula | `13 of 19` LAC-equivalent gap not measured here | — |
| 🟢 EMEA | schools already deployers under the Act | high-risk duties not binding until `2027-12-02` | 🟡 structural |

🔴 **The honest limit:** only the LATAM figure has **both limbs from one source**. The others pair an
adoption claim from one publisher with a governance count from another, which is a weaker
construction, and `598-G` applies to all of them.

### 🟢 `T610-4` — the sector's licence risk has moved from copyleft to *bespoke commercial*

🔵 This base has spent hundreds of passes on the copyleft boundary — GPL in Moodle, AGPL in Open edX,
ECL across Apereo, `CC BY-NC` on the highest-starred research packs (`p601`). 🔴 `610-A` is a
different shape: a **bespoke paid-commercial grant on a fork of an MIT project**, naming MIT four
times while permitting nothing deployable, and reported by the API as `NOASSERTION`.

| era | the risk | what catches it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🔵 copyleft | reciprocal terms propagate to the deliverable | an SPDX id is usually enough |
| 🔴 **non-commercial research** (`p601`) | `CC BY-NC` on the most-starred rows | reading the family, not the stars |
| 🔴 🆕 **bespoke commercial fork** (`610-A`) | a proprietary layer over a permissive upstream | 🔴 **reading the grant body, and then the upstream's** |

🟢 **Why it matters now and not earlier:** agent connectors are small, forkable and cheap to relicense.
`610-A`'s fork is `4,446 KB` against a `105 KB` upstream. 🔴 The fork is what trends; the upstream is
what is usable.

### 🟡 `T610-5` — discovery has decoupled from promotion, and the gap is the asset

🔴 `2,310` addresses are published as rows on this base's trending shelves and sit on no sell shelf;
`848` are education-shaped. 🟢 That is not a backlog defect so much as a **standing asset that nobody
is drawing on**: the expensive part — finding and recording the address — is already paid.

🔴 **What would refute it:** if the backlog were mostly stale or irrelevant. Partly true and measured:
ranked by stars its head is general infrastructure refused on relevance, and `3` of `23` candidates
this pass were refused on relevance after a second read. 🟡 So the usable fraction is well under
`848`, and the pass that measures it properly will be worth more than one that promotes from it.

## 🆕 `p609` — reads `2026-11-02` — current trends

### 🟢 T`609`-1 — the field moved from building tutors to measuring whether they teach

This is the pass's load-bearing trend and it is evidenced by repositories, not by a report. Five
independent groups shipped **open, permissive instruments for honest tutor evaluation** within roughly
eight weeks: [`GEMLabHKU/DAS2`](https://github.com/GEMLabHKU/DAS2) (simulated students that game the
system, wheel-spin and go off-task at empirically matched rates),
[`abigail-gh/simlearner`](https://github.com/abigail-gh/simlearner) (neuro-symbolic learner that
fixes **competency bias** with a symbolic mastery gate instead of a prompt),
[`microsoft/StudentSim`](https://github.com/microsoft/StudentSim) (one simulator per student;
behavioral fidelity and guidance responsiveness as separate axes),
[`pchi1606/sherpa-simulated-student-audit`](https://github.com/pchi1606/sherpa-simulated-student-audit)
(a **preregistered audit** of whether a published simulator was valid at all), and
[`shivanireddyk/tutoreval`](https://github.com/shivanireddyk/tutoreval) (deterministic pedagogical
scoring, no network, no key, every score carrying the span that caused it).

🔵 **The shared premise is the trend:** a tutoring response can be factually perfect and
pedagogically useless, and an ITS benchmarked against students who always respond on-task has not
been benchmarked. *"A tutor that looks great against engaged students may fail completely once real
students walk in."* 🟢 For a studio this inverts the usual order: the **evaluation harness is now
available before the tutor**, so a client engagement can open with a measurement of the incumbent
rather than a demo of a replacement.

### 🟢 T`609`-2 — the knowledge-tracing literature turned on its own metrics

Three of this pass's six foundation rows are not models; they are **critiques of how models are
scored**. `pooled-auc-knowledge-tracing` argues pooled AUC *does not identify within-learner
predictive discrimination* and decomposes it across `14` scoring rules. `kt-benchmark-diagnosis`
argues aggregate AUC hides where errors originate and whether a benchmark is **near saturation**,
and estimates per-interaction irreducible uncertainty as the anchor. `knowledge_tracing_benchmark`
rebuilds the comparison **learner-disjoint** with a pinned dataset revision and reports that a GRU
beats PFA by about `0.05` AUC with a seed SD of `0.0008`.

🔵 **Why it matters commercially:** "our model gets `0.78` AUC" is now a claim with a published
rebuttal attached. 🟢 An engagement that quotes a KT metric without a within-learner decomposition is
quoting a number this literature has already addressed, and the rebuttals are **MIT-licensed and
runnable**.

### 🟡 T`609`-3 — interpretability is arriving as a client requirement, not a research preference

`baptiste-alonso/knowledge-tracing-benchmark` excluded DKT **by design** because *"interpretability
was a requirement of the client"* — a French EdTech firm — so only models with readable parameters
were admitted. 🔵 That is the EU AI Act's posture showing up as an architecture constraint at the
procurement stage, in the region where the Act applies, written down in a repository rather than in a
policy memo. 🟢 It is the cleanest artifact this base holds for an EMEA regulated engagement: the
constraint, the seven admissible models, the protocol and the scores, with the client's own data
withheld and a synthetic generator shipped in its place.

### 🟢 T`609`-4 — the integrity boundary is being built into products, not bolted on

A pattern now visible across three passes' rows: `p608`'s `german-course` **disables its AI tutor
during exams**; `obfusca` anonymises exam PDFs **entirely in-browser so nothing is uploaded**;
`wildmind-ai-for-education` puts the tutor *inside* the assignment so the teacher sees what the AI
inserted; and this pass's `Edwisely-VivaSimulator` defaults to **Ollama**, so an oral exam can be run
without a vendor key. 🔵 The design target has shifted from *detecting* misuse to making
attributable use the default path. 🟡 This is consistent with the market harvest's claim that the
pressure has moved from *"how do we stop cheating"* to pedagogy and cost — though note that claim is
aggregator-sourced, while the four repositories are byte-read.

### 🔴 T`609`-5 — discovery breadth, not search terms, is what has been failing

`39` consecutive nils on the mandated global legs, and this pass ran the two shapes side by side:
a broad `in:name,description,readme` query returned `4,827` hits topped by `public-apis`,
`awesome-selfhosted` and `free-for-dev`; a narrow `in:name,description` domain phrase returned **`3`**,
all three education-specific and one of them the missing eval harness. 🔵 **A broad query ranks the
whole of GitHub by stars, and the top of that list does not change week to week** — which is the nil
streak, exactly. 🟢 `9` of this pass's `11` promotions came from queries whose `total_count` was in
single or double digits. For this domain, a small result set is the evidence the query is working.

### 🔴 T`609`-6 — a permissive grant is not evidence that software exists

[`mkhomytsya/MuckeligAgentLMS`](https://github.com/mkhomytsya/MuckeligAgentLMS) claims the *"first
cloud-native LMS powered by autonomous AI agents"* with a built-in xAPI LRS, *"Kubernetes-Native,
production-ready"* — and carries a genuine **Apache-2.0** grant on a real address. It is **`10 KB`
in total**, created and last pushed on the same day. 🔵 Every terms-based gate passes it; only
**repository size** refutes it. 🟢 Size costs nothing on a channel already in use, so it is now run
before the grant on platform candidates — including on this pass's own rows, which measure `22 KB`
to `5,760 KB`.

### 🟡 T`609`-7 — the regional policy layer is diverging in *kind*, not just in pace

🔵 **North America** is accumulating **volume**: `134` bills across `31` states (March 2026), plus
`$169 M` in federal FIPSE awards — so the compliance target is plural and per-state configurability
is a product requirement. 🔵 **EMEA** is accumulating **obligations**: the AI Act in force since
2024-08-01 with AI-literacy duties live since 2025-02-02, updated Commission guidelines for
education, `€25.4 M` in skills grants, `£200 M+` in the UK. 🔵 **APAC** is moving from drafting to
**enforcement** across at least `16` jurisdictions with no prospect of a common framework, alongside
the single largest teacher-certification commitment in the harvest (>`1 M` teachers, Philippines
DepEd with Google). 🔵 **LATAM** has *"no fully mature, harmonized AI regime"* in any country, with
the stated risk being *"a patchwork of pilot policies, vulnerable to being tossed out with each new
administration."*

🟢 **The consequence for a studio is a build-order difference, not a messaging difference:** EMEA
engagements start from the interpretability/eval constraint, North American ones from per-state
configurability, APAC ones from jurisdiction routing, LATAM ones from offline and
connectivity-tolerant delivery.

### 🔴 T`609`-8 — this base's own absence claims need a denominator

The eval tier was recorded as empty for several passes while two permissive, byte-read eval
repositories sat on this base's **trending** shelves, and `50` of `219` ledger addresses are in that
same state today (`609-C`). 🔵 **An absence keyed on the sell shelves is not an absence of the base.**
Carried forward as a standing instrument requirement: an absence claim states which shelves it was
measured over.

## 🆕 `p608` — reads `2026-11-02` — current trends

### 🔴 The trend that contradicts the other trends

Every vendor source this pass describes 2026 as the year AI became **embedded infrastructure** in
education. The OECD's *Digital Education Outlook 2026* says the measured learning gain is not
there: benefits **disappear or reverse when AI access is removed**, with prior-AI-access students
performing **up to 17 % worse** than never-exposed peers, and the report's own framing is
*technology does not equal transformation*. ⚠️ Read via secondary summaries — the OECD PDF and
`digital-skills-jobs.europa.eu` are both unreachable from this session.

🔵 **Hold both at once:** procurement is accelerating while the efficacy evidence is turning
negative for *unbounded* assistance. That is not a reason to stop selling; it is the reason the
**bounded** designs below are suddenly the defensible ones.

### The trends, with what this pass actually found attached

| # | trend | what `p608` measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **From experimentation to governance** — the question is no longer *whether* but *under what conditions* | 🟢 Confirmed by dates, not opinion: NC **Session Law 2026-41** (2026-12-31 / 2027-06-30 / 2028-06-30), EU Annex III → **2027-12-02**, India **Class 3 from 2026-27**, China **effective 2026-07-15** |
| 2 | **Bounded > general-purpose** — education-specific platforms beat generic chat | 🟢 **Strongest repository signal of the pass.** `sproutask` binds every explanation to a **verified textbook pack with source id + PDF page + printed page**; `HaoyanZhang123` states the design rule outright — *judgements rest on recorded evidence rather than on message counts* |
| 3 | **Agentic workflows** — plan a sequence, find resources, draft assessment for teacher review | 🟢 Present, and now with a permissive ingestion layer underneath it: [`Tencent/WeKnora`](https://github.com/Tencent/WeKnora) (MIT) |
| 4 | 🆕 **Attributable AI use replaces AI detection** | 🟢 **New this pass.** [`wildmind-ai-for-education`](https://github.com/dlealvilla/wildmind-ai-for-education) (MIT): *"AI isn't the problem. Invisible AI usage is"* — the tutor lives **inside** the assignment so the teacher sees the prompts and the inserted text. 🔵 This is the architectural reply to the **72 %** integrity figure, and it does not require a detector that does not work |
| 5 | 🆕 **Integrity boundaries compiled into the product** | 🟢 [`german-course`](https://github.com/yashvikram30/german-course) (MIT) **disables the AI tutor during exams** and runs Goethe A1 Modelltests at real timings. 🔵 Expect this to become a procurement checkbox once EU Annex III assessment duties bite in **2027-12** |
| 6 | 🆕 **Deterministic grading beside the model, not inside it** | 🟢 [`axomiya-study-companion`](https://github.com/prayashcloud/axomiya-study-companion) (MIT) marks answers in **plain Python, not through the LLM, "so scores are always exact."** 🔵 The cheapest way to make a grading claim auditable |
| 7 | **Adaptive personalisation** — maturing from promise to proven benefit | 🟡 Shelf effect: a **second** peer-reviewed KT model arrives on the held benchmark surface — [`HiSACKT`](https://github.com/duaabaig/HiSACKT) (MIT, WI-IAT 2025) on `ASSIST2009/2015`, `Algebra2005`, `Statics2011` — so the tier has a challenger, not just a reference impl |
| 8 | **Privacy-by-construction** | 🟢 [`obfusca`](https://github.com/Bradwave/obfusca) (MIT): identity stripped from exam PDFs **in the browser**, nothing uploaded, redaction codes tuned not to corrupt vision/LLM OCR. 🔵 An EMEA-origin answer to a GDPR constraint that needs **no data-residency negotiation at all** |
| 9 | **Low-resource language coverage as the APAC differentiator** | 🟢 **Taiwanese Hokkien** ([`HokkienTranslation`](https://github.com/LeeLanguageLab/HokkienTranslation), MIT, 2× NAACL 2025 demos) and **Assamese** (`axomiya`, MIT). 🔵 Both are *national-board or named-dialect* specific — the opposite of the generic multilingual claim |
| 10 | **Credentials and skills-based pathways** | 🟡 Reported by 1EdTech; **no permissive repository found** on this shelf for credentialing this pass |
| 11 | **Accessibility compelled by deadline** | 🟡 US **ADA** obligations land **April 2026**. 🔴 The matching code is unusable: `PreethamGM04/AccessLearn-` (accessible-format transformation) ships **no license file** |

### 🔴 The counter-trend nobody is selling: `16 %` of this industry's new code has no grant

`13 of 81` repositories enumerated this pass carry **no license file at any of four names on either
branch** — README present, grant absent. The unlicensed rate is now this shelf's **largest single
source of loss**, ahead of copyleft and ahead of irrelevance. The two most painful:

- 🔴 [`wxxxqcy/ai-learning-assistant`](https://github.com/wxxxqcy/ai-learning-assistant) — an
  **end-to-end evaluation framework for education agents** covering tool use, policy and safety.
  The exact tier this base has recorded as missing for many passes. **No license.**
- 🔴 [`NBrangerF/curriculum-standards-breakdown`](https://github.com/NBrangerF/curriculum-standards-breakdown)
  — a structured, searchable index of the **2022 compulsory-education curriculum standards**,
  updated **today**, `14,416 B` README. **No license.**

🔵 **The trend statement:** education-AI is being written faster than it is being licensed. For a
studio, the binding constraint in this industry is shifting from *"does the capability exist"* to
*"did anyone grant it"* — and that is a question a search engine never answers and a byte-read
always does.

### 🔴 Measurement caveat on this pass's own instrument

A naive license probe run blind against the platform tier produced **three false negatives**
(`XBlock` filed ungranted because its grant sits at **uppercase** `LICENSE.TXT`; `moodle` filed
grantless because GPL-3.0 lives at `COPYING.txt`; `sakai` unclassified because **ECL-2.0 contains
zero occurrences of the string `Apache License`**). All three were traps this base had already
registered, and the base's column was right in all three. 🔵 **A license probe under-reports
permissive grants** — in the one direction that costs a studio options.

---

## 🆕 `p607` — `2026-11-02` — **🟢 THE TREND WITH THE MOST BUILD VALUE IS A CONVERGENCE: THREE REGIONS, THREE UNRELATED LEGAL TRADITIONS, ONE OBLIGATION — **PROVENANCE LABELLING** — EACH WITH A DATED INSTRUMENT. 🟢 AND THE SECOND IS A GOVERNANCE INVERSION MEASURED ON BOTH SIDES: IN LATAM, TEACHER ADOPTION (`>50 %`) EXCEEDS INSTITUTIONAL POLICY (`<10 %`) BY MORE THAN `5×`. 🔴 A METHODOLOGICAL TREND, FOURTH PASS RUNNING: THE INSTRUMENTS ARE STILL THE WEAK LINK — AND THIS PASS MEASURED ONE OF THIS BASE'S OWN GATES AT `0.0 %` PRECISION.**

### 🟢 `T607-1` — provenance labelling is the one obligation three legal traditions converge on (high confidence — three dated instruments, three regions)

`T606-2` registered AI-content labelling as *"converging as the one globally common obligation."*
This pass supplies the independent corroboration that claim needed, from a **different region** and a
**different legal instrument**:

| region | instrument | effective | mechanism |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 APAC — India | IT Rules amendment | 🟢 **`20 Feb 2026`** | **prominent labels + provenance markers** on AI-generated content |
| 🟢 APAC — China | AI-content labelling measures | 🟢 **since `1 Sep 2025`** | service-specific binding labelling |
| 🟢 EMEA — EU | AI Act transparency duties, enforcement by the AI Office | 🟢 **since `2 Aug 2026`** | disclosure of AI interaction and synthetic content |
| 🟡 North America | D.C. OSSE 2026-27 policy | `2026-27` school year | 🟡 **prohibited-use list** — adjacent, not labelling: bans AI from grading, discipline, IEP decisions, teacher evaluation |

🔵 **Why this is a trend and not a list.** India's regime is innovation-first and has no general AI
law; China's is service-specific and state-directed; the EU's is a horizontal risk statute. 🟢 **Three
legal philosophies that agree on almost nothing converged on the same technical requirement** — which
is the strongest available signal that it is a durable build target rather than a policy fashion.

🟢 **Consequence for a studio:** a provenance layer — content tagging, model/version attribution,
retention of the generating prompt and source citations — is **the single most region-portable
component in education AI right now.** It is the same artefact in Delhi, Shanghai, Brussels and
Washington. 🔵 Built as `P607-2` in `compose/patterns.md`.

### 🟢 `T607-2` — adoption has inverted governance, and LATAM is where the ratio is measurable (high confidence — both sides of the ratio sourced, same source)

| | figure | source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 teachers in **Chile and Brazil** already using AI tools | 🟢 **>`50 %`** | UNESCO |
| 🔴 institutions **in the region** with formal guidelines | 🔴 **<`10 %`** | UNESCO |
| ratio | 🔴 **>`5×`** | — |

🟢 **The value is that both numbers come from the same source**, so the gap is not an artefact of
reconciling two vendors. `T-605-7` registered *"adoption has outrun strategy"* as a trend; this is
the first time this base can state it as a **ratio** rather than a direction.

🔵 **It corroborates across regions with different numbers but the same sign:** US — `60 %` of
teachers using AI (Gallup) against *most public schools having no formal student AI policy* (Child
Trends / Dept. of Education); EMEA — `67 %` of academics weekly (QS) against `70 %` of institutions
*having or still developing* guidance. 🟢 **Four regions, one direction: the practice is ahead of the
policy everywhere it has been measured.**

🟢 **Commercial reading:** the engagement is not adoption. Adoption already happened, unsanctioned.
The work is **retrofitting policy onto behaviour that is already in the building** — and that reframes
the buyer from an innovation budget to a risk budget.

### 🟡 `T607-3` — the EU calendar is being reported two ways, and the conflict is itself the finding (medium confidence — two sources, direct contradiction, primary channel blocked)

| source class | what it says about high-risk education AI |
| --- | --- |
| 🟢 regulation-tracking coverage | **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (*Digital Omnibus*, in force late July 2026) moves stand-alone **Annex III** high-risk duties to 🟢 **`2 Dec 2027`**; the **AI Office began enforcement `2 Aug 2026`** |
| 🔴 vendor compliance guide | high-risk **education** requirements apply from 🔴 **August 2026** |

🔴 **These cannot both be true**, and this base cannot adjudicate them: `598-G` blocks the Official
Journal and every primary host. 🟢 `T606-1` already recorded the slip from `2 Aug 2026` to
`2 Dec 2027`; `p607` adds the **regulation number** and the finding that **vendor guidance is still
selling the old deadline.**

🔵 **Why that matters more than the date.** A buyer reading vendor material believes it is already
non-compliant. 🟢 That is an *exploitable* information gap in the honest direction: a studio that can
state the calendar correctly, with the instrument named and the `2 Aug 2026` governance/`2 Dec 2027`
high-risk split explained, is immediately more credible than the guidance the buyer has been reading.

### 🟡 `T607-4` — national AI-in-education policy is becoming a *district* obligation, not a ministry document (medium-high confidence — 4 states, one survey, one prohibited-use list)

🟢 The 2026 North American data shows the obligation moving **down** the hierarchy:

- 🟢 **`4` states** (Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia) now require **both** state guidance **and
  mandatory district-level policy adoption** — the duty lands on the district, not the capital.
- 🟢 **`134` bills** across **`31` states**, concentrated on privacy, classroom restriction and
  curriculum.
- 🟢 **D.C. OSSE** shows the *content*: a list of decisions AI may not make (grading, discipline, IEP,
  teacher evaluation).
- 🟡 **New York A 9190** shows the other axis: restriction **by grade level** (grade 9+).

🔵 **Why this is a distinct trend from `T-605-2` (compulsory AI curriculum).** That one is about what
students are taught; this is about **who is liable for the deployment**. 🟢 A per-district policy duty
multiplies the number of buyers by three orders of magnitude and makes the deliverable a **template
plus an assessment**, not a platform.

### 🔴 `T607-5` — a trend inside this base: the instruments are the weak link, and one of this base's own gates measures `0.0 %` precision (high confidence — `45` hits enumerated, `0` correct)

`p606` registered *"three consecutive passes whose central error was in measurement code, not in the
market."* 🔴 **`p607` is the fourth, and this time the defective instrument is a committed gate.**

🟢 This session could execute `python3 ingest/validate-pages.py` — which `605-E` reports as
impossible. It returns findings on `6` files, the largest class being **"placeholder-as-data rows"**:
the defect the run prompt singles out, where a header row like `| Nombre | Repo | Licencia |` ships as
data. Following the norm `605-I` established — **measure a detector before acting on it**:

| | value |
| --- | --- |
| hits from the **committed** predicate (`ingest/validate-pages.py` line `35`), enumerated in full | **`45`** |
| 🔴 genuine header-as-data rows among them | 🔴 **`0`** |
| 🟢 precision | 🔴 **`0.0 %`** |

🔴 **Every one of the `45` is a vertical key-value row** — this base's own dominant table idiom, where
`| licence | 🟢 **AGPL-3.0**, as carried |` has `licence` as a **row label** and real data beside it.
The predicate matches on the first cell alone and cannot tell a label from a header.

🔵 **The classifier used to grade them is stated so the figure is auditable:** a hit is a genuine
defect only if **every** non-first cell is itself a column label. On `45` hits, that fired `0` times.

🟢 **Verdict: the gate as written cannot fire correctly on this corpus.** It is not under-tuned, it is
measuring the wrong thing — and it is plausibly a contributor to `Gap 539-F`'s *"`17` of `66` standing
gates are red."* 🔴 A red gate that is always wrong is worse than no gate, because it trains every
later pass to ignore it. 🔵 Same lesson as `605-I`, arrived at from the opposite direction: that pass
refused to **adopt** a detector at `5.6 %`; this one refuses to **believe** one at `0.0 %`.

### 🔵 Method and grade

🟢 **Supply side, first-hand:** `28 of 28` grant payloads byte-read today at the real default branch,
every one with a per-repo fabricated-path control at 🟢 **`404 14 B`**; `104` existence probes with the
`606-E` verifier (`200` real / `401` fabricated, validated against controls at the top of the pass).

🔴 **Demand side, snippet-grade:** every figure in `T607-1` – `T607-4` and on `intel/market.md` is a
search-snippet, several from vendor blogs, and the primary hosts remain blocked (`598-G`, ninth pass
open). 🔵 Each is labelled 🟡 at the point of use rather than in a footnote.

🔴 **One instrument retraction and one self-caught error, both before publication:** a draft debt
figure of `73` was wrong because it subtracted promotions from debt without checking that every
promotion *was* debt (`3` were not — the real figure is `76`), and a slug this pass **guessed**

## 🆕 `p606` — `2026-11-02` — **🟢 THE TREND WITH THE MOST COMMERCIAL CONSEQUENCE THIS PASS IS A **DEADLINE THAT MOVED**: EU HIGH-RISK EDUCATION AI SLID FROM `2 Aug 2026` TO `2 Dec 2027`, AND THE NEARER DEADLINE NOBODY IS TALKING ABOUT IS `2 Dec 2026`. 🟢 AND A METHODOLOGICAL TREND WORTH NAMING: `3` CONSECUTIVE PASSES WHOSE CENTRAL ERROR WAS IN MEASUREMENT CODE, NOT IN THE MARKET.**

### 🟢 `T606-1` — the EU education-AI calendar, as it actually stands

🔴 The single most-repeated date in education-AI compliance writing — `2 August 2026` for high-risk
education systems — **is no longer correct**, and most published guides still carry it.

| date | what applies | status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `2 Feb 2025` | 🟢 Emotion-recognition ban in education | **in force** |
| `2 Aug 2026` | 🟢 **Art. 50 transparency** duties | **in force now** |
| 🟢 **`2 Dec 2026`** | 🟢 **Machine-readable marking of generated content for systems already on the market** | 🔴 **the near deadline, and the least discussed** |
| 🟡 July 2026 | AI-literacy duty **softened** to *"measures to support"* staff literacy | in force |
| 🟢 **`2 Dec 2027`** | 🟢 Stand-alone **high-risk** education systems (admissions, placement, outcome evaluation, attainment assessment, exam proctoring) — moved by **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** | 🟢 **`13` months of runway** |

🔵 **Why this is a trend and not a footnote:** the deferral converts a compliance emergency into a
design window. Teams that build logging, human-oversight hooks and technical documentation in now
pay a fraction of a 2027 retrofit. Meanwhile `2 Dec 2026` is `~1` month away and applies to systems
**already deployed** — the opposite of the attention each is getting.

### 🟢 `T606-2` — AI-content labelling is converging as the one globally common obligation

Independent of each other, in different legal styles, four jurisdictions now mandate marking
machine-generated content:

| jurisdiction | instrument | from |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🇪🇺 EU | AI Act Art. 50 / marking for existing systems | `2 Aug 2026` / `2 Dec 2026` |
| 🇨🇳 China | Generative-AI and labelling rules | Sept 2025 |
| 🇮🇳 India | amended IT Rules | Feb 2026 |
| 🇰🇷 South Korea | AI Framework Act | Jan 2026 |

🟢 **Consequence for education specifically:** provenance marking on generated items, feedback and
assessment content stops being a differentiator and becomes table stakes. A tutoring or
item-generation pipeline built in 2026 without a provenance field will need one in every major market
except the US.

### 🟡 `T606-3` — "AI literacy for students" is becoming a curriculum object, not a policy document

| jurisdiction | shape |
| --- | --- |
| 🇩🇪 Germany | 🟢 **KMK `KI-Kompetenzrahmen` (16 Oct 2026)** — four competence areas, explicitly **cross-curricular**, plus `22` reflection cards and a competence navigator in **AIS.chat** |
| 🇺🇸 Idaho | **SB 1227** mandates statewide AI-literacy **standards** |
| 🇺🇸 federal | **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act 2026** would amend ESEA to cover AI literacy — 🔴 not passed |
| 🇳🇬 Nigeria | 2025 curriculum reform adds AI + digital literacy; **Naija Teacher AI** targets `1.5 M` teachers by **Q3 2026** |
| 🌏 APAC | China, Japan, South Korea, Singapore all introducing AI-focused curricula |

🔴 **The consistent criticism is the same everywhere:** frameworks define competences and decline to
say what changes in a lesson or an exam. Germany's own commentators say the 16 Oct framework sets no
concrete requirements for integration or assessment. 🟢 **That is the gap a studio sells into** —
turning a competence framework into instruments, rubrics and teacher-facing tooling.

### 🟡 `T606-4` — teacher-facing automation, not student-facing tutoring, is where the public money is

🔵 The 2026 national programmes found this pass are **all** teacher-capacity programmes, not tutoring
deployments: Naija Teacher AI (`1.5 M` teachers), Google.org/Raspberry Pi **Experience AI**
(`24,000` educators → `1.25 M` students), DfE's May 2026 modules (administrative tasks, SEND plan
drafting, business operations), Germany's AIS.chat teacher tooling.

🟢 And the open-source shelf matches: `instructional_agents` (ADDIE course-material generation) and
the `.github`-hidden curriculum estates are teacher-side. 🔵 **The inference for positioning:** a
public-sector education engagement in 2026 is more likely to be funded as *teacher throughput* than
as *student tutoring*, even though tutoring is where the star counts are.

### 🔴 `T606-5` — a trend inside this base, not the market: the instruments are now the weak link

| pass | load-bearing error | where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `p605` | selection predicate wrong about `59 of 400` rows | 🔴 instrument |
| `p605` | frontmatter writer destroyed `region:` on all `10` pages | 🔴 instrument |
| 🆕 `p606` | README harvester corrupted by its own concurrency; asserted a false repository defect | 🔴 instrument |

🔴 **Three consecutive passes.** Meanwhile the corpus findings in those passes
(`604-B` containment at `0.25 %`, `605-I` containment at `0.19 %`, `606-G`'s hidden README rung) all
held up. 🔵 The base's reading of the world is currently **more reliable than its code**, and
