## 🆕 `p605` — reads `2026-11-02`, commit `2026-11-02` — **🟢 THE `604-H` NATIONAL-QUERY FIX TURNED THE APAC NIL INTO FOUR DATED NATIONAL POLICY FINDINGS, AND ONE OF THEM IS THE SECTOR'S FIRST LARGE-SCALE **FAILURE** WITH NUMBERS ATTACHED. 🔴 THE GLOBAL LEGS RETURN THE THIRTY-FIFTH NIL. 🔵 EVERY FIGURE BELOW IS SEARCH-SNIPPET-GRADE (`598-G`) AND LABELLED.**

### 🔴 `T-605-1` — the first *documented* national AI-education failure, and it is the most useful trend on this page

🟢 Every trend this base has recorded for a year has been an adoption curve. 🔴 **Korea supplies
the other half**, and with figures:

| | Korea's AI digital textbook (AIDT) programme |
| --- | --- |
| legal status | 🔴 **downgraded from *textbook* to *optional supplementary material*** |
| adoption, semester over semester | 🔴 **`37 % → 19 %`** (alternatively `4,095 → 1,686` schools) |
| regional spread | 🔴 `98 %` (Daegu) to `8 %` (Sejong) |
| industry response | 🔴 **~`10` publishers suing the government, ~`₩200 bn`**, June `2026` |
| audit findings | 🔴 **skipped pilots, late standards, weak screening controls** |
| what continues | 🟢 AI-focused schools, AI teaching materials, and a **different** textbook set piloted in semester 1 of `2027` |

🔵 **Why it is a trend and not an anecdote:** it is the first instance of a national programme
being **legally reclassified downward** after deployment. 🟢 The three audit findings are
*procurement* failures, not model failures — which means they are the failures a delivery partner
is actually hired to prevent. 🟡 Figures are secondary-outlet grade; the Korea Times, Yonhap and
Ministry of Education primary sources are outside this session's network policy.

### 🟢 `T-605-2` — AI literacy is becoming compulsory curriculum, not optional enrichment

🟢 **China** issued an **AI + Education Action Plan** in spring `2026` from five central
departments led by the Ministry of Education: AI literacy across all school levels and lifelong
learning, integration into local curricula, interdisciplinary teaching, after-school provision,
demonstration schools, and 🟢 **national platforms to deliver AI courses into rural and remote
schools**. Target `2030`. 🔵 Beijing reports **≥ `8` class-hours per student per academic year**
already. 🟢 **Singapore** will give all students in institutes of higher learning **in-discipline
AI competency development from academic year `2027`**. 🔵 **The shared design choice matters more
than the mandates:** both embed AI *inside existing subjects* rather than creating an "AI class".
🟡 China's announcement date is reported variously as `2`, `10` or mid-April `2026`.

### 🟢 `T-605-3` — the teacher-facing copilot ships before the learner-facing tutor

🟢 **Singapore MOE's rollout order is the signal:** an **Authoring Copilot** for lesson and
assessment materials and a **Learning Assistant** platform, both from **January `2026`**, with
lower-primary pupils given **no** AI tools and upper-primary use supervised and gradual. 🔵 The
same ordering appears in this pass's supply: the permissive rows that exist are
**instructor-side** — [`AI-for-Education/fabdata-llm`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/fabdata-llm),
[`glacy/evolutIA`](https://github.com/glacy/evolutIA) (item generation),
[`ljchang/marimo-grader`](https://github.com/ljchang/marimo-grader) (grading),
[`cloudpedagogy/cloudpedagogy-moodle-course-auditor`](https://github.com/cloudpedagogy/cloudpedagogy-moodle-course-auditor)
(course audit, `p604`). 🟢 **Build order follows deployment order: author, assess, then tutor.**

### 🟢 `T-605-4` — "experimentation to governance" is the year's consensus, and the sources converge from different directions

🟢 1EdTech frames `2026` as the shift **from experimentation to governance** — explicit policies
and data boundaries. Tutorflow describes the same move as experimentation → **operational use
inside repeatable workflows**: lesson prep, formative feedback. 🔵 The regulatory record agrees
independently: 🟢 DC's OSSE bars AI from **grading, discipline, IEP decisions and teacher
evaluation**; `4` US states now require districts to *have* a policy; Australia's TEQSA requires
**every** higher-education provider to file a generative-AI action plan; the EU AI Act requires
human oversight that can **override or shut the system down**. 🟢 **Four jurisdictions, one
pattern: the deliverable is now the boundary, not the capability.**

### 🟡 `T-605-5` — the EU's high-risk clock moved, and the sector has not caught up

🔴 Enforcement of the AI Act by the AI Office and national authorities began **`2 August 2026`**.
🔴 But the **Digital Omnibus** — **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, Official Journal `24 July 2026`,
applying from `27 July 2026` — **postpones high-risk obligations to `December 2027`** for
standalone Annex III systems and **`August 2028`** for embedded ones. 🔴 A second source published
this pass still states `August 2026`. 🟢 **Recorded as an unresolved conflict**, with the
Omnibus-dated source preferred on recency and specificity. 🔵 **The planning consequence is the
same either way:** the obligation is unchanged and the window to build human oversight *into* the
architecture is now `12`–`24` months wide instead of closed.

### 🟢 `T-605-6` — purpose-built education platforms are displacing generic assistants

🟢 TeachBetter's reading: **education-specific platforms are replacing generic AI tools**, and
high-cost enterprise-first products fail to scale in budget-constrained schools, pulling demand
toward all-in-one. 🔵 This base's own supply measurement says the same thing from the other side:
the rows that survive promotion are **narrow and domain-anchored** —
[`psychquant/che-ntucool-mcp`](https://github.com/psychquant/che-ntucool-mcp) targets *one
university's* Canvas; [`glacy/evolutIA`](https://github.com/glacy/evolutIA) targets *one course*;
[`bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark) targets *one national
curriculum*. 🟢 **Specificity is the moat, and it is also what makes a repo shippable.**

### 🔴 `T-605-7` — adoption has outrun strategy, and the gap is measured

🟢 UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS, `200` institutions, `19` countries: **`87 %` use AI in at least one
area; `26 %` have a formal AI strategy** — and adoption is driven **bottom-up by individual
academics, researchers and students.** 🟢 QS, for EMEA: **`67 %` of academics and `62 %` of
students** use generative AI weekly, with guidance outnumbering bans ~`2 : 1`. 🟢 Gallup, for the
US: **`60 %` of teachers** use AI tools while **most public schools have no student AI policy**.
🔵 **Three regions, three instruments, one finding: the technology is already inside the
institution and the governance is not.** 🟢 That is the most reliably sellable observation in this
document, because it is the only one three independent surveys agree on.

### 🟡 `T-605-8` — the counter-current, recorded rather than discounted

🟡 HolonIQ expects **selective** acceleration — more weight on demonstrated instructional value
and ethics — and notes countries piloting AI companions **with strict boundaries** (UK, Greece).
🟡 Berkeley Law banned AI on all exams and credited coursework from summer `2026`. 🔴 Korea's
`19 %` floor is the same signal with a number on it. 🟢 **Read together: growth is in governed,
narrow deployments. A forecast of general-purpose AI tutoring at national scale now has a
documented counter-example.**

### 🔵 Method and grade

🔵 `12` legs: `4` mandated global, `4` mandated regional, `4` national (`604-H`'s fix). 🔴 The
global legs returned `0` new repository addresses — **thirty-fifth consecutive nil**. 🟢 The
national legs returned every new address in the pass and all four findings above that are dated.
🔴 **Every demand and policy figure on this page is search-snippet-grade**: `unesco.org`,
`oecd.org`, `iadb.org` and `unu.edu` are outside this session's network policy, re-measured `000`
today against a non-discriminating control (`598-G`, `583-E`). 🟢 Leg-by-leg ledger:
[`../evidence/websearch-legs.2026-11-02-p605.tsv`](../evidence/websearch-legs.2026-11-02-p605.tsv).

## 🆕 `p604` — reads `2026-11-02`, commit `2026-11-02` — three numbered trends (`80`–`82`), each with the measurement that produced it

## 80. A licence API is not a licence channel, and the gap is concentrated in sector-specific licences (high confidence — `91` repositories, `61` grants byte-read, `90.9 %` failure on one licence)

🔴 **The claim.** Automated licence detection is accurate on the licences it was built around
and **near-totally blind to sector-specific ones**. The failure is not diffuse data quality —
it is a narrow, nameable blind spot, which makes it both more dangerous and far cheaper to fix.

🟢 **The measurement.** All four Apereo-family GitHub orgs, enumerated via
`search/repositories`, every grant byte-read at the real default branch with a fabricated-path
control at `404`:

| | Apache-2.0 rows | ECL-2.0 rows |
| --- | --: | --: |
| repositories carrying this grant (byte-read) | `44` | `11` |
| 🟢 API `spdx_id` agrees | 🟢 **`44` (`100 %`)** | `1` (`9.1 %`) |
| 🔴 API returns `NOASSERTION` | 🟢 `0` | 🔴 **`10` (`90.9 %`)** |

🔵 **The two columns are the finding.** The same classifier, in the same orgs, on the same
day, is **perfect** on the mainstream licence and **wrong nine times out of ten** on the
sector's own — which is a blind spot, not a reliability problem.

🔵 **Why it generalises past education.** **ECL-2.0 is Apache-2.0 with the patent grant
narrowed to education use** — a sector rewriting a mainstream licence for its own needs. Every
regulated sector does this. 🔴 The lesson is that the *more* a sector has invested in its own
legal instruments, the *less* an off-the-shelf licence scanner can see its software. 🟡 For a
services firm this inverts the usual assumption: the sector-specialist stack is the one most
likely to be wrongly blocked by the client's own compliance tooling.

🟢 **What to do with it.** A dependency gate reading `spdx_id` should treat `NOASSERTION` as
**"read the file"**, never as "unlicensed". The body-scan phrase (`educational community
license`) is one line and recovers `9` repositories here. 🔴 A title-line pattern does **not**
work: of `11` ECL repos the parenthetical `(ECL-2.0)` appears in one.

## 81. A grant file is a payload, not a fact, and it fails in at least four distinguishable ways (high confidence — four specimens, one sweep)

🔴 **The claim.** Treating "has a LICENSE file naming X" as equivalent to "is licensed X" is
unsafe, and the failure modes are enumerable rather than endless. One `91`-repo sweep produced
four distinct ones.

| # | failure mode | specimen | what a naive reader returns |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 🔴 the payload is **not a licence** — a saved HTML page of *another repo's* licence | [`uPortal-Project/FeedbackPortlet`](https://github.com/uPortal-Project/FeedbackPortlet) (`11,620 B`) | 🔴 `Apache-2.0` (`Apache License` ×`4` inside the markup) |
| 2 | 🔴 **two grants, two holders**, the second below the first | [`apereo/java-cas-client`](https://github.com/apereo/java-cas-client) (`2,321 B`) | 🔴 `Apache-2.0` only; misses BSD-3-Clause from `JA-SIG, Inc.` |
| 3 | 🔴 **the first line is not the licence name** — a rule, a BOM, or no title | `java-cas-client` (`====`), [`apereo/dotnet-cas-client`](https://github.com/apereo/dotnet-cas-client) (UTF-8 BOM), [`otacke/h5pxapikatchu`](https://github.com/otacke/h5pxapikatchu) (no title) | 🔴 nothing, or unresolved |
| 4 | 🔴 the grant is **someone else's project**, copied verbatim | [`uPortal-Project/CoursesPortlet`](https://github.com/uPortal-Project/CoursesPortlet) — byte-identical to `NewsReaderPortlet`'s, holder names the wrong project | 🔴 `BSD-3-Clause`, as if granted by this repo's owner |

🔵 **The unifying control is cheap:** check the first `512 B` for markup, keep reading past the
first declaration, and compare the holder named in the grant against the repository's owner.
🟢 **Three of the four were caught by reading bytes this base had never read**; the fourth was
already held, which is how it could be used as a positive control.

🔵 **And one shape is worth separating out because it is not a defect at all:** a grant written
in the **language of its region**. `portabilis/i-diario`'s licence opens `LICENÇA PÚBLICA
GERAL AFFERO GNU` and the API returns `NOASSERTION` for it. 🔴 An English-anchored matcher
(`GNU Affero General Public License`) does not match it either. 🟢 Held here since `p319`, and
re-confirmed this pass — it is the clearest case that **licence detection has a regional bias**,
and that the bias falls on exactly the regions a global firm is trying to serve.

## 82. Support status is a README fact, not a push-date fact (medium-high confidence — one clean specimen, one general mechanism)

🔴 **The claim.** Recency metrics — `pushed_at`, last commit date, commit frequency — do not
measure whether software is *supported*, and the discrepancy is systematic rather than random:
**the commit that makes a repository look maintained is often the commit that deprecates it.**

🟢 **The specimen.** [`ucfopen/lti-template-flask-oauth-tokens`](https://github.com/ucfopen/lti-template-flask-oauth-tokens):

| signal | reads |
| --- | --- |
| last push | 🟢 `2026-07-20` — recent |
| grant | 🟢 MIT, `1,129 B`, byte-read |
| publisher | 🟢 a university open-source org with `65` repositories |
| 🔴 README line 1 | 🔴 **`# DEPRECATED` — *"This template is no longer supported"*** |
| 🔴 named successor | [`ucfopen/cookiecutter-python-lti`](https://github.com/ucfopen/cookiecutter-python-lti) — **already held by this base** |

🔵 **Every quantitative signal was green.** 🟢 The only channel that carried the truth was the
**first screen of prose**, and it also named the replacement — so reading it both prevented a
false row and pointed at the correct one.

🟢 **What to do with it.** Read the README's first screen for
`DEPRECATED` / `no longer maintained` / `superseded` / `archived in favour of` **before**
grading recency, and when a successor is named, check whether it is already held. 🔵 This is
the inverse of the usual worry about stale repositories: the risk is not that an old repo looks
dead, it is that a **dead repo looks alive**.

## 🆕 `p603` — reads `2026-11-02`, commit `2026-11-02` — three numbered trends (`77`–`79`), each with the measurement that produced it

## 77. A mature knowledge base's web-search legs become a regression test, and its own backlog becomes the discovery channel (high confidence — 33 consecutive nils against 46 promotions from one re-probe)

🔴 **The claim.** Past some maturity, the mandated demand-side legs stop finding anything, and
a pass that spends its budget there will report `0 new` and be correct. The marginal
information has moved to **re-probing what the base already claimed but never shelved**.

🟢 **The measurement.**

| channel | spend | yield |
| --- | --- | --: |
| 🔴 `8` mandated web legs (`4` global + `4` regional) | the whole specified research budget | 🔴 `45 of 45` tokens held, **`0` new** — the **33rd** consecutive nil |
| 🟢 re-probe of the `p436` promotion backlog | `48` repositories, `raw` + git protocol | 🟢 **`46` promoted**, `3` disconfirmed, `2` rows struck as non-repositories |

🔵 **Why the nil is informative rather than embarrassing.** No region returned *silence* —
every leg came back with dated, sourced, substantive material. 🟢 **The legs are working;
the base is simply ahead of them.** On LATAM policy it is measurably ahead: `p602` recorded
the base holding `Santiago, 2026-04-14` where the leg returned only *"April 2026"*.

🟡 **What to do with it, stated as a specification change rather than a complaint.** Keep the
legs — they are a cheap nil-check and they would catch a genuine market break. 🔴 **But stop
sizing a pass's expected yield from them.** A base with a `167`-pass-old backlog of
`108` claimed-but-unshelved addresses has its next `46` findings sitting in its own
`evidence/` directory, already paid for once.

🔵 **Transfers directly to every other industry KB on this account**, because the rotation
runs the same eight legs per industry and each one accumulates the same kind of backlog.

## 78. Every single-spelling probe is a silent coverage hole, and fixing one channel does not fix the others (high confidence — `5.7 %` of repositories measured invisible on the README channel, after the licence channel was fixed)

🔴 **The claim.** This base learned, expensively and repeatedly, that filenames vary in case,
extension and language (`LICENCE`, `license.txt`, `docs/License.txt`). 🔴 **It fixed that on
the licence channel and left the identical assumption standing on the README channel** — in
the same file, `40` lines apart.

🟢 **The measurement** (`106` repositories with a README, probed `2026-11-02`,
[`../evidence/readme-spelling-ladder.2026-11-02-p603.tsv`](../evidence/readme-spelling-ladder.2026-11-02-p603.tsv)):

| channel | rung | payout rate | in the ladder? |
| --- | --- | --: | --- |
| licence | `LICENCE` (British) | `1 / 44` | 🟢 yes — bought deliberately |
| 🔴 README | `readme.md` | 🔴 **`3 / 106`** | 🔴 **no** |
| 🔴 README | `README.rst` | `2 / 106` | 🔴 no |
| 🔴 README | `Readme.md` | `1 / 106` | 🔴 no |
| | 🔴 **total invisible** | 🔴 **`6 / 106` = `5.7 %`** | |

🔴 **The compounding part.** The README rung is not cosmetic — it is the *fallback* the
licence ladder depends on, the one the library's own header credits with finding
`OS4ED/openSIS-Classic`'s GPL-2.0 at `docs/License.txt` *"in one request"* where no filename
ladder would. 🟢 **So a hole in the README channel silently weakens the licence channel that
was already fixed**, and the repository it hides is exactly the awkward kind the fallback
exists for. 🔵 `kitschpatrol/yanki` defeats both at once: `license.txt` **and** `readme.md`.

🔵 **The generalisation worth carrying.** A ladder is not a property of a *file*, it is a
property of a *channel*, and channels do not inherit each other's fixes. 🟡 **When a
single-spelling assumption is found and fixed, the cheap next move is to grep the same
instrument for every other hardcoded filename** — not to wait for each one to be discovered
by its own incident.

## 79. A loose address matcher does not merely mis-measure a corpus — it injects fictitious work that then persists for as long as the ledger does (high confidence — 2 of this base's own file paths carried as promotion candidates for 167 passes)

🔴 **The claim.** `p602` established that too *narrow* an address matcher understates a held
set. 🔴 **The opposite error is worse in kind, not merely in sign**: too *loose* a matcher
writes non-addresses into a work queue, where they are indistinguishable from real tasks and
are re-counted every pass.

🟢 **The measurement.** `evidence/promotion-ledger.2026-10-25-p436.tsv`, `slug` column:

| row | what it actually is |
| --- | --- |
| 🔴 `evidence/p333-disposition-ledger` | a real local file — `evidence/p333-disposition-ledger.2026-10-20-p333.tsv` |
| 🔴 `evidence/p333-silent-random-probe` | a real local file — `evidence/p333-silent-random-probe.2026-10-20-p333.tsv` |

Both graded `UNRESOLVED` on a `HEAD` symref probe for the obvious reason, and both had sat
typed `BACKLOG` since `p436`. 🔵 **`heldset.sh`'s own header predicted this exact failure** —
*"a bare `<word>/<word>` match hits every relative path in the prose (`compose/code`,
`intel/market.md`)"* — 🔴 **but predicted it as a measurement error, so the control was
placed on the matcher and never on the ledger.**

🟢 **The corrected denominator, which is the practical output:**

| | |
| --- | --: |
| 🟡 `p602`'s stated backlog | `44` |
| distinct ledger slugs typed `BACKLOG` | `142` |
| 🟢 shelved between `p436` and `p603` | `32` |
| on no curated shelf | `110` |
| 🔴 not repositories | `2` |
| 🟢 **real repository backlog** | **`108`** |

🔵 **Why the sign of the error decides where the control belongs.** An under-counting matcher
produces *missed* work — bounded, and self-correcting the next time the address appears. 🔴
**An over-counting matcher produces *invented* work, which is unbounded and self-sustaining:**
nothing in a backlog ever proves a row false, so a fictitious row survives every pass that
does not probe it. 🟢 **A work queue therefore needs a liveness check at admission, not just
a correct matcher** — one `ls-remote --symref` per row at write time would have caught both
in `167` passes' worth of one second.

🔵 **Third mechanism, same family, found in this pass's own instrument:** a novelty probe
graded `ilias-ebuch/ilias` as `NEW` — 🔴 **an address `p603` invented** from a prose platform
name, while the real `ilias-elearning/ilias` has been held since `p191`. 🟢 **Rule:
only ever grade an address a source actually printed.** A prose-named platform with no
printed slug is a lookup, never a `NEW`.

## 🆕 `p602` — reads `2026-11-02`, commit `2026-11-02` — three numbered trends (`74`–`76`), each with the measurement that produced it

## 74. A knowledge base's novelty figure is a property of its address matcher, not of the market — and the error runs toward false novelty (high confidence — two independent instruments, both wrong in the same direction, `39.5 %` of the held set invisible to one of them)

🔴 **The claim.** A pass that reports "`n %` of what I found is new" is reporting its matcher. This
pass ran two novelty instruments and **both** overstated novelty.

🟢 **The measurement.**

| instrument | variant | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| held-set matcher | 🔴 `github.com/<o>/<r>` only | `3,679` addresses |
| held-set matcher | 🟢 URL **+ backticked bare slug** | **`6,083`** |
| | 🔴 **invisible to the first** | **`2,404` (`39.5 %`)** |
| novelty reported | 🔴 first | `359 of 653` (`55.0 %`) |
| novelty reported | 🟢 corrected | **`329 of 653` (`50.4 %`)** |
| demand-side phrase probe | 🔴 exact-phrase `grep -F` | `1` false `NEW` of `41` |

🔵 **Why the direction matters more than the magnitude.** An under-counting matcher produces *missed*
supply — cheap, recoverable next pass. 🔴 **An over-counting novelty figure produces published
findings that are restatements**, which is the expensive failure: `6` rows and `7` claims were drafted
here as new that this base already held, including `version.php` source-header grants at `56` prior
occurrences. 🟢 **Rung: run two matchers, take the larger held set, and treat a bare `<word>/<word>`
match as the opposite error — it swallows every relative path in the prose.**
Evidence: [`../evidence/held-set-matcher-correction.2026-11-02-p602.tsv`](../evidence/held-set-matcher-correction.2026-11-02-p602.tsv).

## 75. A discovery backlog is not a discovery gap — in this sector the LMS-connector category was found, graded and left unshelved for ~`165` passes (high confidence — `44 of 265` ledger rows LMS-shaped, `2,450` addresses graded first-hand at `p435`, `gate-promotion-ledger` red throughout)

🔴 **The claim.** This base's standing instinct on a thin shelf is "sweep harder". 🔴 **For the
LMS-connector category that was the wrong diagnosis for a very long time**: the addresses were already
in hand.

🟢 **The measurement.** [`../evidence/promotion-ledger.2026-10-25-p436.tsv`](../evidence/promotion-ledger.2026-10-25-p436.tsv)
carries `265` rows, 🔴 **`44` of them LMS/MCP-shaped** — `EduardoKrausME/moodle-local_mcp`,
`Teci-AI/moodle-local_mcpconnector`, `aryankeluskar/canvas-mcp`, `NiccoloSalvini/mcp-blackboard-ucsc`,
four separate `classroom-mcp` forks — every one *claimed on a `trending` page* and 🔴 **on no live
shelf**. `p435` had already graded the whole `2,450`-address unpromoted surface first-hand and found it
`59.1 %` permissive. `gate-promotion-ledger` has been red on exactly this, now at `154` findings.

🔵 **Mechanism: discovery and adjudication are different jobs, and only one of them has a gate that
fires.** Sweeping produces visible output; promoting requires a judgement per row. 🟢 **So the backlog
grows silently while the sweep looks productive.** `p602` promoted `5` rows out of it — 🟡 **a first
instalment against `44`, not a closure.**

## 76. An on-topic, permissive, well-populated axis can still be worthless in two distinct ways that narrowing cannot fix (moderate-to-high confidence — `60` axes, `726` rows; `topic:lti` and `topic:university` measured)

🔴 **The claim.** This base already handles substring collisions (`P831`: `tooLTIp`, `muLTI-tenancy`)
and homonym drift (`p601`: `library-management`). 🟢 **Two further mechanisms are measured here, and
neither yields to the existing fixes.**

🟢 **(i) `topic:`-level polysemy.** `topic:lti` returns **Learning Tools Interoperability** *and*
**Linear Time-Invariant**: [kmilo17pet/qlibs](https://github.com/kmilo17pet/qlibs) (`104★`, embedded
control), [jrgenerative/fixed-wing-sim](https://github.com/jrgenerative/fixed-wing-sim) (`164★`),
[dimigeorgiou/Python-Digital-Signal-Processing-Basics](https://github.com/dimigeorgiou/Python-Digital-Signal-Processing-Basics).
🔵 **Word-bounding cannot help** — both communities apply the tag *correctly*. Only pairing the axis
with a sector term does.

🟢 **(ii) Genre capture.** `topic:university` is on-topic and is owned by **internship job boards**:
[SimplifyJobs/Summer2027-Internships](https://github.com/SimplifyJobs/Summer2027-Internships)
(`48,262★`), [speedyapply/2027-SWE-College-Jobs](https://github.com/speedyapply/2027-SWE-College-Jobs)
(`9,592★`). `topic:lecture-notes` is owned by courseware. 🔴 **Narrowing does not fix this** — the
dominant genre is correctly tagged and simply out-ranks everything. The axis must be abandoned or read
far past page one.

🟡 **Confidence held below the other two trends:** both mechanisms are established on `2` axes each,
and "genre capture" is a naming of a pattern this base has probably hit before without labelling it.
🟢 **The practical rung is cheap and independent of the taxonomy: read the top `5` of any new axis
before trusting its relevance rate** — both mechanisms are visible at `n=5`.
Evidence: [`../evidence/axis-pollution.2026-11-02-p602.tsv`](../evidence/axis-pollution.2026-11-02-p602.tsv).

## 🆕 `p601` — reads `2026-11-01`, commit `2026-11-02` — four numbered trends (`70`–`73`), each with the measurement that produced it

## 70. In education, star rank is an anti-signal for commercial usability — the sector's biggest artefacts are courseware, and courseware picks NonCommercial (high confidence — `29` `NOASSERTION` grants byte-read, top `4` by stars all `BLOCK`, `4 of top 5`)

🔴 **The measurement.** `29` repositories whose GitHub licence field reads `NOASSERTION` were byte-read
at the branch `git ls-remote --symref` reports, each with its own fabricated-path control at `404`.
**`28 of 29` (`96.6 %`) resolved to a readable grant.** Of those `28`: `11` permissive, 🔴 **`11`
`BLOCK`** (`7` NonCommercial CC, `2` all-rights-reserved, `2` bespoke), `4` reciprocal, `1`
attribution-only content. 🔴 **Ranked by stars, the top four are all `BLOCK`:**
`Imbad0202/academic-research-skills` (`54,881★`, CC BY-NC), its codex sibling (`13,872★`, same
`19,584`-byte grant), `TheOdinProject/curriculum` (`13,200★`, CC BY-NC-SA),
`AhmadHassan-BTed/Turnitout-Humanizer` (`11,200★`, proprietary).

🔵 **Why it generalises rather than being a sampling accident.** The mechanism is economic, not
statistical: the largest repositories in *education* are teaching material, and an author who wrote a
course chooses `NC` to stop someone reselling their course. 🟢 **The operational consequence inverts
the default heuristic** — in this sector, a shortlist ranked by stars is ranked roughly by *decreasing*
usability, so the grant must be read before the shortlist exists, not after it is presented.
🟡 **Bound:** measured on `29` rows in one sector. It is a strong rule for education and should not be
exported to other industries without its own measurement.

## 71. A discovery nil and a discovery flood can be the same market: novelty has two causes with opposite value, and one number cannot separate them (high confidence — `864` addresses, `71.1 %` new, `1.27 %` shippable, per-axis relevance measured)

🟢 **The funnel, every denominator attached:** `864` addresses from `30` job-phrased topic axes →
**`614` new** (`71.1 %`, screened by `grep -ril` over every `*.md`/`*.tsv` in the repository) →
`273` education-relevant by description keyword (`44.5 %`) → 🔴 **`11` permissive **and** `≥ 300★`
**and** pushed since `2025-06` (`1.27 %` of addresses).**

🔴 **The two causes, measured side by side and indistinguishable on novelty alone:**

| axis | % new | % in-domain | shippable | cause |
| --- | --: | --: | --: | --- |
| `academic-writing` | 🟢 `100 %` | `40.0 %` | 🟢 **`7`** | **genuine unqueried category** |
| `library-management` | 🔴 `100 %` | 🔴 `6.7 %` | `0` | **homonym** — dependency managers |
| `timetable` | `96.6 %` | 🔴 `7.1 %` | `0` | **homonym** — transit, rotas |
| `plagiarism-detection` | `66.7 %` | 🔴 `5.0 %` | `0` | **homonym** — code-clone detection |
| `gradebook` | 🔴 `0 %` | — | `0` | 🟢 **genuine saturation — `30 of 30` held** |

🔵 **So `p599`'s `0 %` and `p600`'s `80.3 %` are both correct and neither is a supply statement.**
`p600` concluded the productive axes are the ones "phrased as jobs, not technologies"; 🟢 **this refines
it — job-phrasing is necessary and not sufficient, and the discriminator is whether the job word is
*exclusive to education*.** A word another industry also uses (`assessment`, `admissions`,
`curriculum`, `timetable`, `library-management`) imports that industry's supply as false novelty.
🟡 **Limit stated against this pass's own instrument:** the relevance column is a description-keyword
proxy with real false negatives — `academic-writing` scores `40 %` because its repositories say
*paper*, *journal*, *thesis*, words the screen lacks — so `44.5 %` is a **lower bound**.

## 72. Open-source school administration is a structural absence, not a search failure — the cleanest in-domain axis in the sector yields zero shippable platforms (high confidence — `88.9 %` in-domain, `18` unheld addresses, `0` shippable)

🔴 **`topic:school-management` is `88.9 %` education-relevant — the highest clean score measured across
`30` axes — and returns `0` platforms that are permissive *and* substantial *and* maintained.** The
population: `ketanchoyal/Our-E-School` at `504★` (MIT, 🔴 last pushed `2021-08-19`), then `17`
repositories between `43` and `138★`, mostly PHP/Laravel/Flask/MERN coursework, a majority last pushed
before `2025`.

🔵 **Why the measurement matters more than the absence.** An absence found by one query is a search
result; 🟢 **an absence found on an axis that is demonstrably unambiguous and well populated is a
property of the sector.** The credible open anchors remain reciprocal or defective in their grants —
`frappe/education` (🔴 a `19`-byte `license.txt` that *names* GPL-3 without granting it),
`OpenEduCat` (LGPLv3, Odoo-coupled), `ERPNext` (GPL-3). 🟢 **SIS/ERP stays buy-or-build**, and this is
now evidenced rather than asserted — the oldest standing gap on `verticals/solutions.md`, confirmed
with its population attached.

## 73. Agent-native course authoring is forming now, in public, at under `35★` (low-to-moderate confidence — `6` repositories, `5` pushed inside one `72`-hour window, `2 of 5` grants unread)

🟢 **Converging architecture, not a single project:** `model-systems-labs/latent` (`2★`, Apache-2.0,
*"build your own learning platform with agents"*), `martinkozle/course-harness` (`0★`, MIT,
**local-first and source-grounded**), `Phineas1500/Onhand` (`32★`, Apache-2.0, in-page QA over set
texts), `AegeanWorks/NeuralDeck` (`12★`), `better-vault/better-vault` (`12★`), together with
`p600`'s `LearnAIHubC/LearnDeck` (`26★`, MIT). 🔵 **All six treat the authoring step as an agent and
the grounding as the institution's own material** — the opposite of the generate-from-the-model-weights
shape that dominated `2025` courseware tooling.

🔴 **Why the confidence is deliberately low, and what would raise it.** Five of six are under `35★`,
`2 of 5` grants are still unread, none is production-ready, and a `72`-hour cluster is exactly what
hobbyist churn also looks like. 🟢 **Falsifiable test, recorded so a later pass can settle it:**
re-measure in `10` passes — **if no member has crossed `500★`, this is churn and this trend should be
struck;** if any has, the category is real and belongs on `agents/top.md`.
🟡 **`topic:lesson-planning` sits in the same band** — `100 %` unheld, entire population `0–5★`,
every member created in `2026` — and it is where this pass's only LATAM-placed and only
sub-Saharan-African addresses came from, so the band is worth watching for *placement* even while its
code is unshippable.

## 🆕 `p600` — `2026-11-01` — a saturated index is usually a saturated *query*, and the thing that hides supply is a branch name

## 66. A discovery nil measures the index you built, and the variable that decides it is per-axis depth — not the ranking function (high confidence — same six axes, same ordering, `39` addresses against `334`, `0 %` against `41.3 %`)

`p599` walked `6` topic axes star-ranked, took `39` addresses, found `0` new, and concluded the
star-ranked channel was exhausted. It then switched to recency ordering, found `3 of 20` new, and
filed the ordering as the cause. 🔴 **The experiment confounded two variables**: changing the ordering
also re-samples the axis. This pass separated them — **same six axes, same star ordering, `334`
addresses instead of `39`: `138` new, `41.3 %`.**

🔵 **And only `2 of the 6` axes are genuinely saturated** (`topic:ai-tutor` `0.0 %`, `topic:lms`
`3.3 %`); the other four pay `20 %`–`65 %`. 🔴 **The saturated axis is the one that sorts first in
every sweep and has been mined hardest for `599` passes** — so a shallow read of a list that *begins*
with the exhausted axis manufactures a convincing nil.

**What to do with it commercially:** when an index reports a market as "covered", the cheap test is
not a different search engine or a different ranking — it is **the same query read deeper, and more
query *shapes***. `28` axes this base had never walked returned `80.3 %` unheld
(`1,322 of 1,646`). 🔵 **Nine of those axes returned `100 %` unheld, and every one of them is phrased
as a job rather than a technology**: `teacher+copilot`, `learning+path+agent`, `lesson+plan+generator`,
`curriculum+agent`, `student+agent`, `ai+grading+agent`, `topic:courseware`, `topic:homework-helper`,
`topic:education-platform`. 🔴 **An index built on technology nouns goes blind to the verbs**, and the
verbs are what a client actually names in a brief.

## 67. Supply can be hidden by a default branch name, and the biggest permissive estates are the likeliest to hide that way (high confidence — `81` repos, `59` on `release`, `0` held in `599` passes)

[`MathWorks-Teaching-Resources`](https://github.com/MathWorks-Teaching-Resources) publishes **`81`**
BSD-3-Clause undergraduate STEM curriculum modules. 🔴 **`59 of 81` (`72.8 %`) default to a branch
named `release`** — not `main`, not `master`. A grant probe that tries `main` then `master` resolves
nothing for `72.8 %` of the org and reports it as unlicensed; a discovery sweep keyed to education
topics never reaches it at all, because the repos carry subject and tooling topics instead.

🔵 **Two independent concealment mechanisms stacked on the same asset**, which is why `599` passes
missed it. 🟡 **And the asset has a real limit that belongs in the same sentence as the find:** the
content is permissive, the MATLAB/Simulink runtime is commercial — these are **course materials a
studio may modify and rebrand, not an open software stack.**

**Generalisation worth carrying:** branch-name diversity tracks *publisher type*, not repo age.
Consortium and vendor-education publishers release from `release`, `19.0`, `develop` or a version
number because their branch model follows a product release train. 🔴 **A `main`/`master`-only
instrument is therefore biased precisely against institution-grade publishers** — the ones an
enterprise engagement most wants to cite.

## 68. A `200` on a branch path is evidence the branch exists and no evidence it is the default — and the mirror rule was never screened (high confidence — `master` returned `200` on `6 of 8`, wrong default on `4 of those 6`, fabricated-branch control `404` on `8 of 8`)

This base established long ago that a `404` on one path is not a dead repository. 🔵 **The inverse was
assumed rather than tested.** Measured: `master` served a real `LICENSE` on `6 of 8` education
platform repos, and on `4 of those 6` the actual default branch is `main`, `develop` or `19.0`. Stale
`master` branches survive renames, so they answer `200` indefinitely.

🟢 **The controls are what make this usable**: a fabricated branch name returned `404` on `8 of 8`, so
the channel is not blindly permissive — it really is serving files from real, non-default branches.
🔵 **Operational rule: resolve the branch with an oracle that reports the default (`git ls-remote
--symref`, or `search/repositories`.`default_branch`), then read the grant. Never infer the branch
from which path answered.**

## 69. A gate that returns zero is indistinguishable from a world that contains nothing, so a zero must be proved reachable before it is recorded (high confidence — one unimplemented metacharacter turned `66` candidates into `0`, in the same pass that refuted a twenty-eight-pass nil)

This pass's first relevance filter over `678` new addresses reported **`0`**. The cause was a
word-boundary metacharacter that `awk` in this environment does not implement: `/\bai\b/` matches
nothing and raises nothing. Re-run without it: **`66`**.

🔴 **The failure mode is that a broken filter and a saturated market produce byte-identical output** —
and this base had just spent twenty-eight passes misreading exactly that kind of output as a fact
about the industry (trend `66`). 🔵 **So the rung is not "test your regexes"; it is structural: any
screen whose result is `0` must first be shown capable of returning non-`0` on a known positive.**
A nil is a measurement only when the instrument has been demonstrated to be able to fail to produce it.

**Commercial reading:** this is the cheapest possible due-diligence step on any "we found nothing"
report — vendor, internal or automated. 🔵 **Ask what the instrument returns on a case that is known
to be present.** If nobody ran that control, the finding is not a finding.

---

## 🆕 `p599` — `2026-11-01` — a discovery nil can be an artefact of the RANKING FUNCTION, and the bespoke non-commercial licence lives in the band nobody audits

## 62. A star-ranked view of a market returns the history of whoever built the index, so twenty-eight passes of "nil" measured this base and not the industry (high confidence — same channel, two orderings, `0 %` against `15 %`, measured in the same minute)

Six `github.com/topics/<t>` heads were walked star-ranked (the default ordering) and every address on
each first page was checked against this entire base before being called new: **`39` addresses,
`39` already held, `0` new — `0.0 %`.** The *same* topic page, re-requested with
`?o=desc&s=updated` so it ranks by push date instead of stars, served **`20` addresses of which `3`
this base has never held — `15 %`.**

🔵 **The two numbers differ by one query string.** This base has been assembled from star-ranked
views for `598` passes, so a star-ranked view is a query against its own history; the nil it returns
is a *self-measurement*. 🔴 **Twenty-eight prior passes read that nil as a property of the market
("the industry is saturated") or of the channel ("search is broken"). Both readings are refuted by
the `15 %`.**

🔴 **And the qualifier that stops this becoming an over-correction:** all `3` new addresses are
`≤ 4★`. The ordering that reaches unheld supply reaches it **only below this base's shelf floor**.
🟢 **So the defensible statement is the narrow one: what this base lacks is not simultaneously
findable and shippable.** Neither "saturated market" nor "broken channel" is true; the index and the
shelf floor are measuring the same boundary from opposite sides.

**Operative consequence.** 🔵 **Discovery budget should be spent on orderings, not on more queries.**
A nil must name its ordering before it is recorded, and a nil reported from a single ordering
certifies nothing. Registered as **`599-B`**.

## 63. The hand-written "looks open" licence concentrates in the sub-`5★` tail, which means a star floor acts as an accidental licence filter — and removes the audit that would catch it (high confidence — `1 of 3` on the only unheld cohort this base has measured, and the matching instance at `2,057★` took five passes to catch)

Of the `3` genuinely-unheld addresses measured this pass, **`1` carries a bespoke non-commercial
grant**: `2092372408-pixel/EduPilot`, `2★`, `LICENSE` byte-read `1,951 B`, titled
*"EduPilot Noncommercial Source License 1.0"* and opening *"All rights reserved"*. Its build manifest
reads `"license": "SEE LICENSE IN LICENSE"`.

🔵 **The mechanism is attention, not intent.** A `2★` repository has no downstream consumers, so
nobody has ever had a commercial reason to read its grant; a hand-written licence with a plausible
title survives there indefinitely. At scale the same shape gets caught — but slowly:
[`PageLM`](https://github.com/CaviraOSS/PageLM) carried a *"PageLM Community License"* that opens
with MIT's permission sentence verbatim, and this base published it as **MIT** for five passes at
`2,057★` before `p594` re-graded it.

🟢 **The counter-intuitive consequence for a shelf policy.** A star floor is normally justified as a
*quality* filter. Measured here it is also doing **licence** work: it excludes the band where
bespoke non-commercial grants live. 🔴 **But the same floor removes the only population that would
generate an audit trail**, so the shape re-enters the shelf the moment a repository of this kind
grows. 🔵 **The rule that follows: the licence screen must be hardest at the *top* of the shelf, not
the bottom** — exactly inverse to where a star floor concentrates scrutiny — because a high-star
bespoke grant has already passed every social check that would have caught it.

**Corollary, measured.** 🔴 **A build manifest cannot close this gap.** `p598` added the manifest as
a fourth grant channel; on this instance it returns `"SEE LICENSE IN LICENSE"` — 🟢 an *honest*
pointer, so the channel does not mis-grade, but its yield on the unheld tail is `0 of 1`.
Registered as **`599-C`**.

## 64. Between firms, a market forecast's `2026` base is reproducible and its endpoint is not — so the quotable number and the impressive number are different numbers (high confidence — five firms, bases inside a `1.56×` range, endpoints spread `4.3×`)

Five research firms sized AI-in-education for `2026` this pass: `$7.30 B`, `$9.58 B`, `$10.4 B`,
`$10.6 B`, `$11.4 B` — a `1.56×` spread, and **every one inside the `$7–11.4 B` band this base has
published for five passes.** Their endpoints: `$32.27 B` (`2030`) to `$136.79 B` (`2035`) — a
**`4.3×`** spread, with CAGRs from `25.9 %` to `41.5 %`.

🔵 **The base is an observation and the endpoint is an assumption**, and only the assumption is
proprietary to the firm. One of them (Precedence) states the mechanism in its own methodology note:
the figures *"depend on market boundaries and the treatment of embedded AI revenues"* — and
*embedded* revenue is the term that compounds, so boundary choices that barely move `2026` dominate
`2035`.

🔴 **This partially reverses a rule this base adopted at `p93`** ("never quote a market size without
naming the scope"), which was derived from a scope failure — a regional series summing to `$2.07 B`
against the same band. 🟢 **Both are true at different time horizons: scope breaks the comparison
*across* a firm's own products; growth assumption breaks it *between* firms at the endpoint.**
🔵 **Deck rule: quote the `2026` base and cite two firms; if an endpoint is unavoidable, name the
firm, year and CAGR in the same sentence.** 🔴 **And reject the `$12.5 B` `2026` figure now
circulating in vendor blogs — it exceeds every research firm measured.** Registered as **`599-E`**.

## 65. A classification gate fails on transposed tables unless it requires that EVERY cell be header vocabulary, not that one cell is (high confidence — `23` false positives against `0`, same base, same minute)

The header-as-data gate — the run prompt's one explicit quality bar — was re-derived this pass.
`p590`'s form (a header-vocabulary row not followed by a `|---|` separator) returns **`23` hits on
this base and all `23` are false positives**: every one a *transposed* key-value table whose
left-hand column is a row label (`| Licencia | MIT |`, `| Repo | [slug] |`).

🔵 **The discriminator `p590` was missing is arity.** A genuine header-as-data defect is a row in
which **every** cell is drawn from header vocabulary (`| Nombre | Repo | Licencia |`); a transposed
row has **exactly one** such cell and the rest are values. Requiring `cells >= 2 AND
cells == header-vocabulary cells` returns **`0` hits and `0` false positives** across all `9` pages.

🔵 **The general law, which is why this is a trend and not a chore:** a gate written against the
*shape* of a defect will fire on any structure that shares that shape. `p590` published its gate
*with* its false-positive count, which is what made this correction cheap — 🟢 **so the practice
worth keeping is publishing the false-positive count, not the gate.** Registered as **`599-D`**.

---


## 🆕 `p598` — `2026-11-01` — the sector's own licence is the one the tooling cannot read, and the unit of sale is becoming the country

## 58. A licence screen built on `spdx_id` rejects the education sector's own licence, and therefore most of its institution-grade software

🟢 **Measured this pass, `9` repositories, every grant byte-read.** ECL-2.0 — the **Educational
Community License**, Apache-2.0 with the patent grant narrowed to the needs of education consortia —
is OSI-approved and permissive. The GitHub API reports it correctly for
[`sakaiproject/sakai`](https://github.com/sakaiproject/sakai) and 🔴 **wrong for `6 of 8` measured
Apereo-family siblings**, which come back `NOASSERTION`.

🔵 **The discriminator is cosmetic.** Sakai's licence title line carries the parenthetical SPDX tag
`(ECL-2.0)`; `OpenLRW`, `OpenLRS` and `OpenDashboard-legacy` carry the same licence without the
parenthesis and are graded as having no assertion at all.

🔴 **Why this is a trend and not a trivia item.** Apereo is where universities put their shared
infrastructure — Sakai (LMS), uPortal (portal), CAS (the SSO a large share of university estates
run), OpenLRW (learning-record warehouse). It is the deepest pool of **permissively licensed,
institution-grade** education software that exists, and an automated licence gate throws most of it
out. 🟢 **The sector-specific inference:** in education, `NOASSERTION` means *"probably permissive,
open the file"* far more often than *"no grant"*. 🔵 Any studio running an OSS intake screen on
`spdx_id` alone is systematically under-counting exactly the software an education engagement needs.

## 59. A missing `LICENSE` file is not evidence of a missing grant — but the correction is worth less than it looks

🟢 **The finding.** [`Apereo-LAI/SakaiXAPI-Provider`](https://github.com/Apereo-Learning-Analytics-Initiative/SakaiXAPI-Provider)
has **no grant file on `16` filename variants** at its real default branch, and the API says
`NOASSERTION`. It is nonetheless **ECL-2.0**, declared in `pom.xml`. Maven, npm, Cargo and Python
projects can carry their only licence declaration in the **build manifest** — a channel this base
probed `0` times in `597` passes.

🔵 **And the measured yield, which is the half a trend report usually omits.** All `16` of this
base's 🔴 "no grant" BLOCK rows were re-probed across `10` manifest formats: **`1 of 16` (`6.3 %`)**
declares a licence, and that one is `0★`. 🟢 **`15 of 16` BLOCK grades were correct.** The
instrument gap was real and it had put **no wrong data** on these pages.

🔴 **The asymmetry that keeps it from being a promotion rule.** A manifest field is *weaker*
evidence than a grant file: `"license": "MIT"` is a metadata string with no licence text and no
copyright holder. It establishes **intent**, not a grant. 🟡 Correct disposition: **REVIEW**, never
🟢. 🔵 The honest summary — *a new channel worth adopting for the rows where it is the only channel,
not a reason to re-grade a shelf.*

## 60. The unit of sale in education AI is shifting from the school to the country

🔵 **Three independent 2026 signals of one shape.** OpenAI launched a country-level education
programme with **eight national partners** in Q1 2026. The Philippines' Department of Education,
with Google, is certifying **more than `1 million` teachers** in Gemini under Project S.I.N.A.G.
Forrester expects **`60 %` of firms and governments in markets such as India and China** to run
locally developed LLMs alongside global ones, naming education among the target sectors.

🔴 **What it changes for a studio.** If national infrastructure deals displace school-by-school and
district-by-district procurement, the addressable work moves away from *building the tutor* and
toward **integrating, governing and migrating a national stack the client did not choose**. 🟢 That
favours interop capability (roster and LMS standards, MCP attach points), policy and governance
scaffolding, and migration tooling — and it devalues a bespoke student-facing tutor, which is also
the category North American district demand is **declining** in (`p597`).

🟡 **Grade:** snippet-level sourcing on all three signals (`598-G`); the *shape* is corroborated
three ways, the individual figures are not primary-verified.

## 61. In education the governance gap is the engagement, and it is a 25-point regional spread

🟢 **The comparative figure, served this pass:** `~70 %` of institutions in Europe and North America
have or are developing AI guidance, against `45 %` in Latin America and the Caribbean.

🔵 **Read as sales motion rather than as a statistic**, this is three different first deliverables
in three regions. **EMEA** has a statutory deadline — the EU AI Act's AI-literacy duty, in force
since `2025-02-02` — so the entry point is literacy and compliance. **North America** starts inside
an existing institutional policy and a `134`-bill, `31`-state patchwork, so the entry point is
integration that survives audit. **LATAM** frequently has no policy at all, and Baker McKenzie finds
**no Latin American country with a fully mature harmonised AI regime** plus pilot policies *"tossed
out with each new administration"* — so the entry point is **writing the governance scaffold**, and
it should be built as configuration rather than code because the policy will change.

🔴 **APAC does not fit the spread**, and the gap is declared rather than filled: the legs have now
failed across several passes to return an APAC school or university adoption rate. Its organising
constraint is measured instead on the sovereignty axis — which, usefully, is the strongest argument
in any region for the self-hosted permissive stack this KB curates.

## p597 — 2026-11-01 — the verification instrument this harness is told to use cannot distinguish a live repository from a dead one, and the two "nil" channels it reports are independent

## 55. The host you verify at decides the answer, and the host named in the instructions is the one that cannot answer

🔴 **Measured this pass:** `curl -sI https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>` returns **`403`** for every
repository not attached to this session — not `404` for a dead address and `200` for a live one, but
`403` for both. 🔵 The run prompt's standing instruction is *"verify every URL before writing it
(`curl -sI`). A 404 is not a finding."* 🔴 **Executed literally in this environment that instruction
rejects every true row**, because it never sees a `404` *or* a `200`; `20` live, permissive,
institution-grade repositories were promoted this pass and all `20` answer `403` at that host.

| instrument | `jupyterhub/jupyterhub` (live) | `DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents` (live, **unindexed**) | `nonexistent-org-xyz99/nope-12345` (truly dead) | separates live from dead? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `curl -sI github.com/…` | 🔴 `403` | 🔴 `403` | 🔴 `403` | 🔴 **no — one answer for all three** |
| GitHub search API, `repo:` qualifier | 🟢 full record | 🔴 **`total_count 0`** | 🟢 `total_count 0` | 🔴 **no — live-but-unindexed is indistinguishable from dead** |
| GitHub search API, `user:` listing | 🟢 listed | 🔴 **absent from all `15` indexed repos of that owner** | 🟢 absent | 🔴 **no** |
| `curl raw.githubusercontent.com/…` | 🟢 `200`, `1,475 B` | 🟢 `200`, `1,067 B` | 🔴 `404` | 🟢 **yes — `28/28` live, `404` on the control** |
| `WebFetch https://github.com/…` | 🟢 page renders | 🟢 `490★`, `106` forks, MIT | 🔴 no page | 🟢 **yes** (and it works where `curl` gets `403`) |

🔴 **`597-F` — the correction this pass had to make against itself, mid-pass.**
[`DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents`](https://github.com/DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents) was written
into three pages of this base **as a dead address** on the strength of `total_count 0`. 🟢 It is
live: `490★`, `106` forks, MIT (`1,067 B`, byte-read), an **EACL'26 Main** peer-reviewed multi-agent
instructional-design system, with its package published on PyPI. 🔴 **It is simply absent from the
GitHub search index** — `user:darl-genai` lists `15` repositories and this is not one of them, while
four *siblings* (`instructional_agents-skills`, `instructional_agents_studio`,
`instructional_agents_homepage`, `MASkills`) **are** indexed. 🔵 Search-index membership is not
existence, and a `0` is not a `404`.

🔵 **A wrong explanation was also drafted and withdrawn.** The first hypothesis was case-sensitivity
in the `repo:` qualifier, since the canonical owner is `DaRL-GenAI` and this base's census
lower-cases every address. 🔴 **Refuted by measurement:** `repo:DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents`,
`repo:darl-genai/instructional_agents` and `repo:DARL-GENAI/INSTRUCTIONAL_AGENTS` all return
`total_count 0`. 🟢 Had it been published, it would have sent the next pass to re-case `1,449`
addresses for nothing.

🔴 **The search API has its own false negative:** it returns `422 "cannot be searched either because
the resources do not exist or you do not have permission"` for
[`digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet`](https://github.com/digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet),
which is alive and served a `1,092 B` MIT grant from `raw.githubusercontent.com` on the same pass.
🔵 **A `422` reads as "dead" to any screen that only checks for success.**

🟢 **The standing prescription:** liveness and grant are established at `raw.githubusercontent.com`
or they are not established. The GitHub API is a metadata *enrichment* (stars, push date, default
branch, archived flag), never the existence test, and `github.com` itself is not an instrument here
at all.

## 56. A repository's declared licence field and its actual code grant are different objects, and they diverge in at least three structurally different ways at once

🔴 This pass byte-read `21` grants and found the API's `spdx_id` wrong in **three distinct shapes**
inside a single sitting — not three instances of one bug:

| shape | instance | API says | the code grant is | the trap |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Two grant files, one for prose and one for code** | [microsoft/autogen](https://github.com/microsoft/autogen) | 🔴 `CC-BY-4.0` | 🟢 **MIT** — `LICENSE-CODE`, `1,141 B`, root + `7` package trees | The API is *correct* about `LICENSE` (`18,650 B`, genuinely Creative Commons) and *useless* for reuse. A compliant screen **rejects** a `61,661★` MIT framework |
| **A grant with no title line** | [crewaiinc/crewai](https://github.com/crewaiinc/crewai) | 🟢 `MIT` | 🟢 **MIT** — `1,055 B`, opens `Copyright (c) 2025 crewAI, Inc.` | A title-matching byte-reader returns `UNRECOGNISED`; the grant must be identified by its **operative clause** (`Permission is hereby granted, free of charge…`), not its heading |
| **A grant filed under a national spelling** | [oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem](https://github.com/oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem) | 🟢 `MIT` | 🟢 **MIT** — the file is **`LICENCE`**, `1,086 B` | A probe enumerating `LICENSE`/`LICENSE.md`/`LICENSE.txt` reports **no licence** for a national body's MIT repository |

🔵 **The three failures point in opposite directions**, which is why no single fix covers them: the
first needs the *file set* enumerated, the second needs the *contents* parsed rather than the title,
the third needs the *filename* spelled both ways. 🟢 A probe must do all three and must report which
file it read and how many bytes, so a later pass can tell a measurement from an assumption. 🔴 **This
base recorded "AutoGen (MIT)" in two earlier cycles and was right by luck**, never having opened the
file.

## 57. Saturation is a per-channel property, and an undifferentiated "nil" cannot tell a saturated channel from a broken gate

🔴 For `27` consecutive passes this base reported "nil" across its search legs and read that as a
fact about the education industry. `596-C` then found the gate was consulting append-only logs that
never forget. 🟢 **Rebuilt this pass to read the shelf and the logs as separate answers, the single
"nil" split into two opposite results:**

| channel | measurement | verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Market / demand-side tokens** | `22 of 24` tokens held **on the shelf itself**; `1` new; `1` log-only | 🔴 **The nil is real** — twenty-eighth consecutive pass. A genuinely saturated channel |
| **Addresses / supply-side** | `2,640 of 3,366` ever-shelved addresses (`78.4 %`) carry no current disposition; **`2,049`** are reported "held" by a log-grepping gate | 🟢 **The nil was an artifact** — `18` real rows promoted this pass without running a single new search leg |

🔵 **The two channels of one industry saturated independently**, and the aggregate statistic was the
average of a true finding and a false one. 🟢 **The prescription is that a nil must name its
channel** and must state which page class it was gated against; a bare "no new findings" is not a
measurement. 🔴 It also means the fix for one channel buys nothing in the other: correcting the gate
recovered `20` supply-side rows and changed the demand-side result by **one token**.

## p596 — 2026-11-01 — twenty-seven passes of "nil" was not measuring the industry or the search channel; it was measuring this base's own refusal to file what it had already found

## 52. In education's platform-of-record tier the binding rule is not "copyleft with one exception" but "permissive platforms exist and are invisible to a star-based screen", because the permissive ones are microservice estates whose weight is spread across hundreds of repositories (high confidence — one platform measured at organisation level, `456` repositories, `0` copyleft)

`p584` established this industry's platform tier as *copyleft with exactly one permissive
exception*, and `p588` added a second. 🔵 **Both were found by looking at repositories.** This
pass looked at an **organisation** and the rule weakened again.

| `org:folio-org` | repos |
| --- | --: |
| total | **456** |
| 🟢 `license:apache-2.0` | **398** (`87.3 %`) |
| 🔴 any copyleft (`gpl`/`agpl`/`lgpl`) | 🟢 **`0`** |
| 🟡 no licence field — auxiliary tooling, CI, docs, Helm | `57` |

🟢 **FOLIO is a full system of record** — patrons, inventory, circulation, acquisitions, ERM —
and a studio may fork it, modify it and ship it closed. 🔴 **Its largest single repository is
`okapi` at `134★`, and its inventory module — the actual system of record — is `4★`.** 🔵 **So
any screen that ranks platforms by stars on one address rates a live, Apache-2.0,
institution-grade platform below a `2,000★` single-repo demo.** That is how FOLIO sat in this
base's trending logs across several passes without ever reaching a shelf.

🔴 **The operational sting is on the deployment path.** The `57` unlicensed repositories are
not platform code, but **`folio-org/folio-helm` is among them** — so the platform is
permissively licensed and the most common way to deploy it carries no grant at all. 🔵 Cheap
to fix, and it has to be known before procurement rather than after.

🟢 **Instruction this trend carries:** for the platform tier, screen on **organisation-level
licence uniformity and liveness**, never on one repository's stars.

## 53. A permissive licence and a real corporate owner are now jointly insufficient to clear an education repository, because the major clouds publish education AI as explicitly disclaimed proof-of-concept work — and the disclaimer excludes the population the tool is built for (high confidence — one instance measured first-hand, and it clears every other screen this base runs)

🔴 [`GoogleCloudPlatform/aira`](https://github.com/GoogleCloudPlatform/aira) — *Artificial
Intelligence Reading Assessor*, Apache-2.0 byte-read at `11,357 B`, `24★`, live, not archived,
authored by Google Cloud education engineers. 🟢 **It clears licence, liveness, provenance and
relevance — every screen this base runs.**

🔴 **Its README disqualifies it**, and the terms are not boilerplate:

| declaration | consequence |
| --- | --- |
| 🔴 *"an open-source **demo experiment**"* / *"**proof-of-concept only**"* | not a base to build on |
| 🔴 *"**not an officially supported Google product**"* | the brand carries no support obligation |
| 🔴 *"Please do not use any personal or sensitive data"* | cannot touch learner records |
| 🔴 *"should not be used by children under **13**"* | 🔴 **excludes K-12 — the population a reading assessor exists for** |

🔵 **Why this is a trend and not an anecdote.** A permissive licence on a cloud vendor's
education demo is a *marketing* artefact, not a support commitment, and the gap between the
two is widening as the clouds publish more education AI reference work. 🔴 **A licence screen
cannot see it.** The disqualifying text is in the README, in prose, and an under-13 exclusion
on an education tool is categorical rather than a caveat.

🟢 **Instruction: read the README's own status and audience declarations before promoting any
row, and treat a PoC or under-13 disclaimer as a `BLOCK` regardless of grant and
provenance.** Registered as `596-D`.

## 54. When a knowledge base pairs an append-only discovery log with periodically rewritten curated pages, and gates new findings against the log, every row the rewrite drops becomes permanently unrecoverable — and the base reports a saturated market (high confidence — `3,174` addresses classified against `15` archived shelf snapshots, `2026-11-01`)

🔴 Measured this pass with a collation-free instrument, against the `15` shelf snapshots under
`archive/` named `pre-reset`, `pre-rewrite`, `pre-trim` and `pre-consolidation`:

| population | count |
| --- | --: |
| addresses in the ledger | `3,174` |
| 🔵 logged on the two append-only trending pages | `3,143` |
| 🟢 on a **current** shelf page | **`234`** |
| 🔵 on **any** shelf page ever | `2,243` |
| 🔴 **dropped** — shelved once, absent from every current shelf | 🔴 **`2,015`** (`89.8 %` of ever-shelved) |
| never shelved anywhere | `921` |
| lines on the trending pages vs the shelf pages | `115,968` vs `6,516` |

🔴 **Two storage disciplines with opposite loss behaviour, and the base treats them as one.**
The trending log is append-only and forgets nothing. The shelves are rewritten wholesale and
have lost `89.8 %` of everything ever placed on them.

🔴 **The failure is in the coupling, not in either half.** `569-B` requires every candidate to
be grepped against this base before being called new — and that grep hits the **log**. 🔴 **So
a row dropped by a rewrite still answers *"already held"*, forever, and can never be recovered
by searching.** The row is simultaneously absent from the shelf and permanently ineligible for
re-entry.

🟢 **The symptom is indistinguishable from market saturation**, which is how this base read it
for twenty-six passes. 🔵 **The diagnostic that separates them is cheap**: compare the curated
pages against the archived snapshots, not against the log. `6` rows were recovered that way
this pass, none of which needed a search leg.

🟢 **It also corrects `595-A`.** `p595` registered `118 MB` and no retention policy as a size
problem. 🔴 **The `118 MB` is the only part of this base that never loses anything**; the
expensive defect is the rewrite that trims the shelf. 🔵 A retention policy that prunes the log
would remove the one durable record and leave the lossy half untouched.

## p595 — 2026-11-01 — twenty-six passes of nil is no longer a research result about the industry; it is a measurement of the channel, and the cost of the record is now itself the finding

### The legs, measured against this base before being called new

All four mandated regional legs ran, plus the four global legs and one topic axis
(`grading / autograder / rubric`). Every token was grepped against this base before being
called new — `569-B`'s standing requirement.

| token served | region | already in this base? |
| --- | --- | --- |

🔴 **`13 of 13` already held — no region returned a token this base lacks.** This is the
**twenty-sixth** consecutive nil on the mandated legs, and it is now a measured property of
the channel rather than a run of bad luck. 🟢 **Stated as the prompt requires: the gap is
not regional silence.** Every region answered, and every region answered with this base's
existing picture. The four global legs returned `DeepTutor`, `OpenMAIC`/`MAIC` (held, `148`
files), `ai-agents-for-beginners`, `CrewAI` and `AutoGen` — **zero new addresses**.

### 🔴 `595-A` — a standing defect nothing in this base has ever recorded: it has no retention policy

Measured this pass with `du`, and grepped first to confirm it is unrecorded
(`repo size` → `0` files, `bloat` → `0`, `archive policy` → `0`):

| path | size |
| --- | --: |
| `compose/` | **53 MB** |
| `archive/` | **31 MB** |
| `evidence/` | **17 MB** |
| `agents/trending.md` | **5.3 MB** (`65,414` lines) |
| `repos/trending.md` | **4.0 MB** (`50,386` lines) |
| `README.md` | **2.7 MB** |
| `Makefile` | **144 KB** |
| **working tree total** | **118 MB** |

🔴 **The append-only rule that protects the trend record has no counterpart that bounds
it.** At `~595` passes the two trending pages are `115,800` lines between them; a reader
looking for this week's signal pages through fifty thousand lines of history to find it, and
a `git clone` of a single-industry KB now moves `118 MB`.

🔵 **This is a finding, not an action.** Pruning history would destroy the trend record the
append-only rule exists to preserve, so this pass did **not** truncate anything. What is
needed is a *retention decision* — most plausibly: keep the newest `N` passes inline and
roll older sections into `archive/` with an index — and that decision belongs to whoever
owns this KB, not to pass `595`.

## p594 — 2026-11-01 — a licence rule adopted mid-life does not re-grade the shelf behind it, and the two rules this base adopted three passes apart are mutually destructive

## 50. In an append-only curated base, adopting a classification rule silently creates two cohorts — rows graded under the new rule and rows still carrying the old grade — and the old cohort is where the dangerous errors live (high confidence — mechanism identified and one live instance found at `2,057★`)

`p591` adopted `591-A`: classify a licence on its **title**, not its body, because two candidates
(`ximing/aimo` → Business Source License 1.1, `SethRobinson/UGTLive` → Proton SDK licence) contained
MIT's permission sentence verbatim while being something else entirely. The rule was correct. 🔴 **It
was applied to candidates arriving after it and never to rows already published.**

**The live instance.** [`CaviraOSS/PageLM`](https://github.com/CaviraOSS/PageLM) (`2,057★`) sat on
this industry's agent shelf marked 🟢 **`MIT (text)`** — under a legend reading *"permissive …
buildable and shippable in client work"*. Byte-read this pass, its `LICENSE.md` is the **"PageLM
Community License"**: non-commercial use only, redistribution *"strictly prohibited"*, commercial
use conditional on a **signed revenue-sharing agreement**, breach of which is *"a material breach
and immediate termination"*. 🔴 **It is not MIT and it is not open source.** The `(text)` mark means
a pass did byte-read the file — and read MIT's opening sentence and stopped.

**Why this generalises past one row.** A curated base that is append-only in its *history* is
mutable in its *pages*, and a rule change touches only what a pass happens to look at. The cohort
boundary is invisible because both cohorts render identically: a 🟢 from `p560` and a 🟢 from `p594`
are the same glyph carrying different amounts of evidence. 🔵 **And this base predicted it in writing.** `591-G`, registered the same pass the rule was
adopted, states that *every `(text)` licence mark filed before `p591` is unauditable* and prescribes
the audit that catches it — a byte-size filter on the claimed licence. 🔴 **It was never run, for
three passes.** 🟢 Run at `p594` over all `99` `(text)` rows it flags `8`, of which `2` are real
mis-grades, `PageLM` among them at a `7.8×` size discrepancy. 🔵 **So the failure was not a blind
spot — it was a known, documented, costed exposure that no pass spent its budget on.** That is the
more uncomfortable finding and the more generalisable one.

🔵 **Three instances of one pattern,
across three passes, with only the two newest caught**, is the signature of rule-without-sweep —
and the signature is visible only from outside the pass that introduced the rule.

**The operative consequence.** 🟢 **When a pass adopts or revises a licence rule it must re-grade
every row already published under the old rule and record the count swept.** A rule with no sweep
attached is a rule that protects future rows and abandons present ones, which is the wrong way
round: the published rows are the ones a reader is acting on today. Falsifiable: a sweep of the
remaining `(text)` rows finding no further mis-grade would bound the damage at one row, and that
sweep has not been run.

## 51. A licence probe has at least five independent axes on which it returns a confident wrong answer instead of an error, and the two corrections this base adopted three passes apart cancel each other out (high confidence — all five demonstrated first-hand on live rows, `2026-11-01`)

`593-B1` named one axis — a probe that hardcodes `main`/`master` receives `404` on every filename
and concludes "no licence file exists". This pass measured that axis across the whole curated base
and found four more beside it. 🔵 **What the five have in common is the only property that matters:
none of them fails loudly.** Each returns a well-formed wrong answer, so none appears in a channel
table as a failure.

| axis | measured this pass | wrong answer produced |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🔴 **default branch** | **`30 of 253`** curated addresses (`11.9 %`) on `18` names other than `main`/`master`; `19` give `404`, and `11` give a **`200` from a branch the project does not ship** | "no grant" — or worse, a stale grant that reads as success |
| 🔴 **filename case** | `frappe/erpnext`, `frappe/lms`, `frappe/education` keep grants at lowercase **`license.txt`**; `NO-GRANT-FILE` from both a `6`- and a `7`-path probe | three live ERP/LMS rows filed unlicensed |
| 🔴 **prose prepend** | `odoo/odoo` (`55,463★`) and `openeducat_erp` open *"For copyright information, please see the COPYRIGHT file."*; the grant (`LGPL-3.0`) is declared on **line 2** | a title-based rule returns nothing |
| 🔴 **grant language** | `portabilis/i-diario`, `portabilis/pre-matricula-digital` → **`LICENÇA PÚBLICA GERAL AFFERO GNU`** | English matcher false-blocks two live Brazilian school systems |
| 🔴 **unanchored abbreviation** | an unanchored `MPL` substring matches **`65 of 65`** grants read, because every licence disclaims warranties *"EXPRESS OR I`MPL`IED"*; `MIT` matches `li`**`mit`**`ation`, `sub`**`mit`**`ted`, and `Technische Hochschule `**`Mit`**`telhessen` | false **permissive** — the worst direction to be wrong in |

**🔴 The collision, which is the finding.** `591-A` says *classify on the TITLE*. `593-B` found
grants **whose title is prose**. Run `591-A` on `odoo/odoo` and it resolves nothing; run a body
match on it and the appended GPL text can return `GPL-3.0` when the actual grant is the **weaker**
`LGPL-3.0` — an answer that is wrong in the restrictive direction and would retire the Odoo-family
recipes this KB is built on. 🔵 **Two corrections adopted three passes apart, each sound on its own
evidence, are jointly insufficient, and neither pass could have seen it** — `591-A`'s cases had
clean titles and `593-B`'s case had prose.

**🟢 The instrument that survives all five.** A three-stage read, in order: **(1) the licence
title; (2) a *"published / licensed / released under X"* declaration in the first 40 lines, which
beats the body; (3) the body, last.** Plus: branch from the API before any path probe, `13`
path/case variants, full licence names or whole-token abbreviations only, and **a title/body
disagreement escalated to review rather than resolved**. 🟢 Run that way against `10` known-hard
rows it returns **zero false permissives**, resolves `odoo` and `openeducat_erp` to `LGPL-3.0` from
their line-2 declaration, resolves the Portuguese AGPL rows, recovers
`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem` as **Apache-2.0** from a blank field, and **refuses to pass `PageLM`**.

🔵 **Why high confidence:** every axis is demonstrated on a named live row rather than hypothesised,
the `30`-row figure is a census and not a sample, and the corrected instrument was validated
against the cases that defeated its predecessors — including the one that defeated this pass's own
first draft (`594-E`). Falsifiable: a sixth axis, or any row where the three-stage read returns a
false permissive.
